# Quiz key register (technical-workforce courses)

Generated by generate_tech_courses.py. One row per scored item for clinical and technical review.

# Dialysis Technician

## Module 1: The Dialysis Unit and the Technician’s Role

| Q | Stem | Keyed answer | Explanation | Objective |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Where should unopened supplies be staged? | Designated clean supply area | A designated clean area separates supplies from used equipment and waste. | Objective 1 |
| 2 | Who authorizes a change to the dialysis prescription? | Authorized prescribing clinician | The prescribing clinician makes medical decisions about treatment parameters. | Objective 2 |
| 3 | Which pair can verify identity when approved by the facility? | Full name and birth date | Both identifiers are person-specific and must be checked against the approved source. | Objective 3 |
| 4 | What does the R in SBAR stand for? | Recommendation | This element states the action or review you are requesting. | Objective 4 |
| 5 | A station was cleaned but the machine failed a pre-use check. What does that mean for readiness? | The machine remains unavailable pending authorized clearance | Cleaning and technical readiness are separate prerequisites. | Objective 1 |
| 6 | Which note is objective? | 07:10 — Patient said, ‘I feel dizzy.’ Nurse notified at 07:11. | It records the reported symptom, timing and action without speculation. | Objective 5 |
| 7 | Which response shows a check-back? | I’ll hold setup until you verify the record. Correct? | Repeating the instruction gives the recipient a chance to confirm or correct it. | Objective 4 |
| 8 | A wrong record is caught before setup. How should it be classified for safety review? | Near miss | The problem was intercepted before it reached the patient. | Objective 5 |
| 9 | The name matches, but the stated birth date differs. What does that mean? | Identity is unresolved and setup stays on hold | Both identifiers must be verified before relying on the record. | Objective 3 |
| 10 | The patient cannot state their identifiers. What should you do? | Ask the nurse to follow the alternate verification procedure | The alternate procedure supplies an authorized basis for verification. | Objective 3 |
| 11 | A caller who knows the surname asks for a patient’s schedule. What should you do? | Use the facility caller-verification and disclosure process | A surname alone establishes neither identity nor authority to receive information. | Objective 5 |
| 12 | A patient reports new chest discomfort during a routine check. What comes first? | Alert the dialysis nurse and follow emergency escalation procedure | New chest discomfort warrants immediate clinical attention. | Objective 4 |
| 13 | A coworker asks you to do a task you have only observed. What should you do? | Clarify authorization and supervision with the preceptor | Observation gives exposure. The preceptor confirms competency and permitted practice. | Objective 2 |
| 14 | You recorded a check under the wrong machine ID. What should you do? | Correct it under the approved procedure and retain an audit trail | An auditable correction makes clear which machine was checked and limits reliance on an erroneous record. | Objective 5 |
| 15 | A patient asks to shorten treatment because of transport. What should you do? | Acknowledge the concern and relay the request and reason to the nurse | The nurse can raise the barrier with the clinical team while treatment decisions stay within the authorized scope. | Objective 2 |
| 16 | Which wording is the correct way to ask a patient to confirm identity? | “Please tell me your full name and date of birth.” | An open question makes the patient supply the identifiers; a yes can be given to any name. |  |
| 17 | Jose Santos and Josefina Santos are scheduled on the same shift. What should happen? | Both records carry a name-alert label and the alert is stated at handoff | Similar names are marked, kept apart where possible, and announced at every handoff. |  |
| 18 | At 10:15 you realize you did not write down the 09:30 reading you took. What is correct? | Write it now, marked as a late entry, with the current time and the time of the observation | A late entry is honest; backdating is falsification. |  |
| 19 | When may cleaning and disinfection of a station begin? | After the patient has left the station | The station is cleaned only once the patient has left it, and the next patient’s supplies come after cleaning. |  |
| 20 | Which statement is a suitable Assessment in a technician’s SBAR report? | “Her blood pressure was 150/90 and is now 96/58, and she says she is dizzy.” | The technician reports observations and numbers, not a cause or a treatment. |  |
| 21 | After treatment, who decides that the patient is fit to go home? | The nurse | The technician measures and reports; the nurse assesses and decides. |  |

## Module 2: Kidneys and Kidney Failure

| Q | Stem | Keyed answer | Explanation | Objective |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Which nephron structure first filters water and small solutes from blood? | Glomerulus | The glomerulus is the nephron’s initial filtration site. | Objective 1. [NIDDK · How the kidneys work](https://www.niddk.nih.gov/health-information/kidney-disease/kidneys-how-they-work) |
| 2 | Which structure carries urine from the kidney to the bladder? | Ureter | The ureter carries urine from the renal pelvis toward the bladder. | Objective 1. [NIDDK · How the kidneys work](https://www.niddk.nih.gov/health-information/kidney-disease/kidneys-how-they-work) |
| 3 | Which hormone signals the bone marrow to produce red blood cells? | Erythropoietin | Erythropoietin stimulates red blood cell production. Reduced kidney production can contribute to anemia. | Objective 2. [NIDDK · Anemia in CKD](https://www.niddk.nih.gov/health-information/kidney-disease/anemia) |
| 4 | What minimum duration supports the definition of CKD when qualifying abnormalities persist? | 3 months | CKD requires evidence of abnormalities present for at least three months. | Objective 3. [KDIGO · CKD guideline](https://kdigo.org/wp-content/uploads/2024/03/KDIGO-2024-CKD-Guideline.pdf) |
| 5 | A diagram shows useful solutes moving from tubular fluid back into blood. What process does this represent? | Tubular reabsorption | Reabsorption returns selected filtered substances from the tubule to the circulation. | Objective 1. [NIDDK · How the kidneys work](https://www.niddk.nih.gov/health-information/kidney-disease/kidneys-how-they-work) |
| 6 | A mock result shows low serum bicarbonate. Which kidney function is most directly related to this finding? | Acid excretion and bicarbonate regulation | The kidneys excrete acid and conserve or regenerate bicarbonate. The clinician interprets the result in context. | Objective 2. [NIDDK · CKD complications](https://www.niddk.nih.gov/health-information/professionals/education-cme/kidney-disease-education-lesson-builder/lesson-3-when-kidney-disease-gets-worse) |
| 7 | A patient with advanced CKD has reduced vitamin D activation and altered phosphate handling. What complication does this help explain? | CKD-related mineral and bone disorder | Impaired phosphate handling and reduced activation of vitamin D can contribute to CKD-related mineral and bone disorder. | Objective 2. [NIDDK · Mineral and bone disorder](https://www.niddk.nih.gov/health-information/kidney-disease/mineral-bone-disorder) |
| 8 | After a recent illness, creatinine rises over two days in a patient already known to have CKD. How should you interpret the timeline? | The pattern may reflect AKI superimposed on CKD | Acute deterioration can occur in someone who already has chronic kidney disease. Report the trend for clinical evaluation. | Objective 3. [KDIGO · AKI guidance](https://kdigo.org/guidelines/acute-kidney-injury/) |
| 9 | Why can a person with kidney failure still pass urine? | Urine volume alone does not show how well wastes and electrolytes are regulated | Residual urine production can persist even when overall filtration and regulation are severely impaired. | Objective 3. [NIDDK · Kidney failure](https://www.niddk.nih.gov/health-information/kidney-disease/kidney-failure/what-is-kidney-failure) |
| 10 | Previous post-dialysis weight was 67.2 kg and current pre-dialysis weight is 69.4 kg. What should you report? | A 2.2 kg gain between the recorded weights | 69.4 minus 67.2 equals 2.2 kg. Report the measured change. The fluid removal prescription is a separate decision. | Objective 4. [DOH · Dialysis facility standards](https://elibrary.judiciary.gov.ph/thebookshelf/showdocs/10/50687) |
| 11 | The same patient now reports new breathlessness at rest. What should you do first? | Alert the dialysis nurse immediately and follow unit emergency procedure | New breathlessness at rest needs prompt clinical assessment. Escalation takes priority over routine calculations. | Objective 4. [DOH · Dialysis facility standards](https://elibrary.judiciary.gov.ph/thebookshelf/showdocs/10/50687) |
| 12 | A verified potassium result is marked critical, but the patient says they feel well. What is the best action? | Escalate promptly through the critical-result pathway | A critical potassium result requires timely clinical review even in the absence of symptoms. | Objective 4. [NIDDK · Kidney failure](https://www.niddk.nih.gov/health-information/kidney-disease/kidney-failure/what-is-kidney-failure) |
| 13 | A new chart contains one low eGFR value. How should you communicate it? | Report the value and date and note that prior results are unavailable | A single abnormal eGFR cannot establish chronicity. Reporting the result and missing history helps the clinical team investigate. | Objective 3. [KDIGO · CKD guideline](https://kdigo.org/wp-content/uploads/2024/03/KDIGO-2024-CKD-Guideline.pdf) |
| 14 | The patient is tired and a previous hemoglobin is flagged low. Which handoff best fits your role? | The patient reports increased fatigue and the prior hemoglobin is flagged low | This reports the symptom and available result objectively without diagnosing the cause or directing treatment. | Objective 4. [NIDDK · Anemia in CKD](https://www.niddk.nih.gov/health-information/kidney-disease/anemia) |
| 15 | Today’s weight was entered as post-treatment, but you measured it at arrival. What should you do before it is used? | Escalate the discrepancy and follow the approved record correction process | Treatment timing changes how the weight is interpreted. Correct it through the unit process and alert the team before it is relied upon. | Objective 4. [DOH · Dialysis facility standards](https://elibrary.judiciary.gov.ph/thebookshelf/showdocs/10/50687) |
| 16 | A patient’s last post-weight was 71.5 kg. Today’s pre-weight is 74.6 kg. What is the interdialytic weight gain? | 3.1 kg | 74.6 minus 71.5 equals 3.1 kg, about 3.1 liters of fluid. |  |
| 17 | A patient with a dry weight of 75.0 kg has gained 3.0 kg. What is the gain as a percent of dry weight? | 4.0 percent | 3.0 divided by 75.0, times 100, equals 4.0 percent. |  |
| 18 | A blood pressure cuff that is too small for the patient’s arm gives a reading that is: | Falsely high | A cuff that is too small overestimates blood pressure; one that is too large underestimates it. |  |
| 19 | A patient in a wheelchair reads 92.6 kg on the platform scale. The empty wheelchair weighs 19.4 kg. What is the patient’s weight? | 73.2 kg | 92.6 minus 19.4 equals 73.2 kg. |  |
| 20 | A hepatitis B surface antigen result is newly reported as reactive for a patient due on the next shift. Why must the nurse know before setup? | Station and machine assignment must be decided first | Infection status decides where and on which machine the patient is treated. |  |
| 21 | A patient’s pulse feels irregular. How should the rate be counted? | For a full minute | An irregular pulse is counted for a full minute and the irregularity is reported. |  |
| 22 | Why can a dangerously high potassium be missed at the chair? | It often causes no symptoms | High potassium can be silent, which is why a flagged result is reported even when the patient feels well. |  |

## Module 3: Treatment Options and Living with Kidney Failure

| Q | Stem | Keyed answer | Explanation | Objective |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | What serves as the exchange membrane in peritoneal dialysis? | The peritoneal lining | The lining of the abdomen provides the surface across which fluid and solutes move. | Objective 1. [NIDDK · Peritoneal dialysis](https://www.niddk.nih.gov/health-information/kidney-disease/kidney-failure/peritoneal-dialysis) |
| 2 | Which option uses an extracorporeal dialyzer at home? | Home hemodialysis | Home HD circulates blood through an external dialyzer using a home machine. | Objective 1. [NIDDK · Hemodialysis](https://www.niddk.nih.gov/health-information/kidney-disease/kidney-failure/hemodialysis) |
| 3 | Which device performs programmed exchanges in APD? | A PD cycler | APD uses a cycler to carry out prescribed exchanges. | Objective 2. [NIDDK · Peritoneal dialysis](https://www.niddk.nih.gov/health-information/kidney-disease/kidney-failure/peritoneal-dialysis) |
| 4 | Which option involves surgically placing a donor kidney? | Kidney transplant | Transplantation places a living- or deceased-donor kidney into the recipient. | Objective 1. [NIDDK · Kidney transplant](https://www.niddk.nih.gov/health-information/kidney-disease/kidney-failure/kidney-transplant) |
| 5 | Which description best fits comprehensive conservative kidney management? | Active symptom-focused and supportive care without dialysis or transplantation | Conservative management is an active care pathway guided by symptoms, quality of life and patient goals. | Objective 1. [KDIGO · CKD guideline, chapter 5](https://kdigo.org/wp-content/uploads/2024/03/KDIGO-2024-CKD-Guideline.pdf) |
| 6 | A patient wants to compare PD and home HD. Which equipment distinction is accurate? | PD uses solution bags and a PD catheter. Home HD uses an external dialyzer and vascular access. | The two therapies use different exchange surfaces and different types of access. | Objective 2. [NIDDK · Treatment comparison chart](https://www.niddk.nih.gov/health-information/kidney-disease/kidney-failure/choosing-treatment/comparison-chart) |
| 7 | A patient assumes a kidney transplant means all kidney-related follow-up ends. What needs clarification? | Ongoing follow-up and anti-rejection medicines are generally needed with a functioning graft | The transplant team monitors graft function and ongoing immunosuppression. | Objective 1. [NIDDK · Kidney transplant](https://www.niddk.nih.gov/health-information/kidney-disease/kidney-failure/kidney-transplant) |
| 8 | A patient says home HD would make travel easier. Which additional point belongs in the discussion? | Training, home setup and backup support are part of assessing whether it will fit | Travel is one factor. The home program evaluates training, setup, practical support and backup arrangements. | Objective 2. [NIDDK · Hemodialysis](https://www.niddk.nih.gov/health-information/kidney-disease/kidney-failure/hemodialysis) |
| 9 | Which patient statement best shows accurate teach-back after approved contact instructions? | If my ride falls through, I’ll call the dialysis unit using this number to ask what to do. | A specific explanation in the patient’s own words shows what action they understood. | Objective 4. [AHRQ · Teach-back](https://www.ahrq.gov/health-literacy/improve/precautions/tool5.html) |
| 10 | Ms. Reyes says she may have no transport for tomorrow’s dialysis. What should you do first? | Alert the dialysis nurse promptly and follow the unit’s attendance pathway | A potential missed treatment needs timely attention. The nurse and unit determine the safe plan. | Objective 4. [Philippine DOH · Dialysis facility standards](https://elibrary.judiciary.gov.ph/thebookshelf/showdocs/10/50687) |
| 11 | The patient asks whether her daughter must be present for every home HD treatment. What is the best response? | Requirements depend on the program and patient circumstances. I’ll direct your question to the home team. | Care-partner requirements vary. The home program can assess suitability and explain its requirements. | Objective 2. [NIDDK · Hemodialysis](https://www.niddk.nih.gov/health-information/kidney-disease/kidney-failure/hemodialysis) |
| 12 | A patient wants to keep working but has rotating shifts. Which question best elicits what matters? | Which shifts are hardest to attend, and what would help you keep working? | An open question identifies the patient’s priorities and gives the clinical team specific barriers to address. | Objective 3. [NIDDK · Choosing a treatment](https://www.niddk.nih.gov/health-information/kidney-disease/kidney-failure/choosing-treatment) |
| 13 | A patient on PD calls and reports cloudy drained fluid and new abdominal pain. What should you reinforce? | Contact the PD team promptly using the program instructions and follow the urgent escalation pathway as indicated. | These symptoms can signal infection and require prompt assessment by the PD team. | Objective 4. [NIDDK · Peritoneal dialysis](https://www.niddk.nih.gov/health-information/kidney-disease/kidney-failure/peritoneal-dialysis) |
| 14 | During teach-back the patient says they will wait silently if they cannot attend. What should you do next? | Restate the approved contact plan in plain language and ask for teach-back again. | Teach-back shows where communication needs repair. Re-explain and check again. | Objective 4. [AHRQ · Teach-back](https://www.ahrq.gov/health-literacy/improve/precautions/tool5.html) |
| 15 | A relative offers to manage treatment discussions, but the patient has expressed a preference to speak privately. What should you do? | Respect the patient’s preference and ask whom they want involved before sharing information. | Shared decision-making centers the patient’s preferences and establishes who may join the discussion. | Objective 3. [KDIGO · CKD guideline, chapter 5](https://kdigo.org/wp-content/uploads/2024/03/KDIGO-2024-CKD-Guideline.pdf) |
| 16 | A session is prescribed for 240 minutes. The patient ends 30 minutes early. What share of the session was delivered? | 87.5 percent | 210 divided by 240, times 100, equals 87.5 percent. |  |
| 17 | A patient with a 1,000 mL allowance has had one coffee (180 mL), one bowl of soup broth (200 mL), and two glasses of water (240 mL each). How much is left for the day? | 140 mL | 180 + 200 + 480 = 860 mL, and 1,000 minus 860 leaves 140 mL. |  |
| 18 | A patient mentions switching to a salt substitute to cut down on salt. Why should the nurse be told? | Many salt substitutes are made from potassium | Potassium-based salt substitutes can raise blood potassium in a person with kidney failure. |  |
| 19 | A patient says, “Take off an extra kilo today, I drank a lot.” Which reply is within the technician’s scope? | “The nurse and doctor set how much comes off. I will tell the nurse what you told me.” | Fluid removal is a clinical decision; the technician passes on the information. |  |
| 20 | A patient says he ate star fruit yesterday and now has hiccups that will not stop. What should you do? | Tell the nurse immediately | Star fruit is harmful in kidney failure, and persistent hiccups can be an early sign. |  |
| 21 | A patient quietly says, “Sometimes I think everyone would be better off if I were gone.” What is the correct response? | Stay with the patient and call the nurse to the chair immediately | Talk of wanting to die is reported at once and is never kept secret. |  |

## Module 4: Principles of Hemodialysis

| Q | Stem | Keyed answer | Explanation | Objective |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Which force primarily drives the diffusion of urea across a dialyzer membrane? | A concentration difference across the membrane | Diffusion moves permeable solutes down their concentration gradient. Urea generally moves from blood toward dialysate. | Objective 1. [UK Kidney Association · Membrane flux and transport](https://guidelines.ukkidney.org/haemodialysis/rationale%20for%20clinical%20practice%20guidelines/membrane%20flux%20and%20haemodiafiltration/) |
| 2 | Which term describes net water movement across the membrane driven by a pressure difference? | Ultrafiltration | The pressure difference across the membrane drives water movement. Controlled net water removal is ultrafiltration. | Objective 1. [UK Kidney Association · Membrane flux and transport](https://guidelines.ukkidney.org/haemodialysis/rationale%20for%20clinical%20practice%20guidelines/membrane%20flux%20and%20haemodiafiltration/) |
| 3 | A dissolved solute crosses the membrane as it is carried along with moving water. Which process describes that solute movement? | Convection | Convection is the movement of permeable solutes with water through the membrane. Ultrafiltration describes the water movement itself. | Objective 1. [UK Kidney Association · Membrane flux and transport](https://guidelines.ukkidney.org/haemodialysis/rationale%20for%20clinical%20practice%20guidelines/membrane%20flux%20and%20haemodiafiltration/) |
| 4 | At one point in a teaching dialyzer, dialysate bicarbonate concentration is higher than blood bicarbonate concentration. In which direction does bicarbonate tend to diffuse at that point? | Toward the blood | A permeable solute tends to diffuse from higher to lower concentration. The direction depends on the gradient for that solute. | Objective 1. [UK Kidney Association · Membrane flux and transport](https://guidelines.ukkidney.org/haemodialysis/rationale%20for%20clinical%20practice%20guidelines/membrane%20flux%20and%20haemodiafiltration/) |
| 5 | Why does countercurrent flow help sustain diffusion in a conventional dialyzer? | It maintains a concentration difference for waste solutes along more of the membrane. | Opposing blood and dialysate flows help maintain usable concentration gradients over the dialyzer’s length. | Objective 2. [UK Kidney Association · Membrane flux and transport](https://guidelines.ukkidney.org/haemodialysis/rationale%20for%20clinical%20practice%20guidelines/membrane%20flux%20and%20haemodiafiltration/) |
| 6 | In a simulated session, the net ultrafiltration goal is zero while blood and fresh dialysate still flow. What can still happen? | Urea removal by diffusion | Urea can still diffuse across the membrane when a concentration gradient is present. Net fluid removal and diffusive solute removal are distinct processes. | Objective 1. [UK Kidney Association · Membrane flux and transport](https://guidelines.ukkidney.org/haemodialysis/rationale%20for%20clinical%20practice%20guidelines/membrane%20flux%20and%20haemodiafiltration/) |
| 7 | What are the usual inputs for preparing conventional bicarbonate dialysate? | Treated dialysis water and the matched acid and bicarbonate concentrates | The system uses treated dialysis water with the correct matched concentrates. Verify the prescribed formulation and equipment compatibility under the facility’s protocol. | Objective 2. [CDC · Water use in dialysis](https://www.cdc.gov/dialysis-safety/hcp/recommendations-resources/water-use-in-dialysis.html) |
| 8 | In the conceptual expression Kt/V, what does t represent? | Treatment duration | K represents urea clearance, t represents time, and V estimates the volume in which urea is distributed. | Objective 4. [UK Kidney Association · Urea clearance](https://guidelines.ukkidney.org/haemodialysis/appendices/appendix%20a/) |
| 9 | A colleague says a urine output estimate is required to calculate the urea reduction ratio (URR) for this session. Which data are needed for the simple URR calculation? | The paired pre- and post-dialysis blood urea nitrogen (BUN) results | URR is the percentage decrease between paired pre- and post-dialysis BUN values. The clinical team may consider residual kidney function separately when evaluating the overall prescription. | Objective 4. [UK Kidney Association · Urea clearance](https://guidelines.ukkidney.org/haemodialysis/appendices/appendix%20a/) |
| 10 | During a simulation, the pre-use concentrate label disagrees with the patient's verified prescription even though the machine displays conductivity within range. What should you do? | Hold setup and notify the authorized nurse or supervisor to resolve the mismatch. | Conductivity does not verify every ingredient or replace label and prescription checks. Resolve the mismatch through the approved process before proceeding. | Objective 2. [NIDDK · Hemodialysis](https://www.niddk.nih.gov/health-information/kidney-disease/kidney-failure/hemodialysis) |
| 11 | The two BUN results for a mock URR both have a pre-treatment timestamp. What should you do before using them? | Flag the discrepancy and ask the clinical team to verify the samples and recorded times. | URR requires verified pre- and post-treatment samples from the corresponding treatment. A mismatched time or label can invalidate the result. | Objective 4. [UK Kidney Association · Dialysis dose](https://guidelines.ukkidney.org/haemodialysis/rationale-for-clinical-practice-guidelines/dialysis-dose-in-thrice-weekly-dialysis-schedules/) |
| 12 | What is the difference between target weight and a prior post-treatment weight? | Target weight is a clinical goal. Prior post-treatment weight is a measurement from the previous session. | The goal and actual measured weight may differ. The clinical team reviews the target using current assessment and treatment history. | Objective 3. [UK Kidney Association · Fluid management](https://guidelines.ukkidney.org/haemodialysis/rationale-for-clinical-practice-guidelines/fluid-in-haemodialysis/) |
| 13 | The prior post-dialysis weight was 64.8 kg and today’s pre-dialysis weight is 67.1 kg. What is the measured interdialytic weight gain? | 2.3 kg | Subtract the prior measured post-treatment weight from today’s measured pre-treatment weight. Here 67.1 − 64.8 = 2.3 kg. | Objective 3. [UK Kidney Association · Fluid management](https://guidelines.ukkidney.org/haemodialysis/rationale-for-clinical-practice-guidelines/fluid-in-haemodialysis/) |
| 14 | In the same case, the target weight in the chart is 65.4 kg and the session’s prescribed ultrafiltration goal is unclear. What is the best next step? | Report the weight values and verify the prescribed goal with the authorized nurse before programming. | The measured gain and the 1.7 kg difference from target answer different questions. Neither independently authorizes a machine setting. | Objective 3. [UK Kidney Association · Fluid management](https://guidelines.ukkidney.org/haemodialysis/rationale-for-clinical-practice-guidelines/fluid-in-haemodialysis/) |
| 15 | A simulation order lists 1,800 mL of fluid removal over 3 hours. Assuming the rate is constant, what is the average ultrafiltration rate in mL/hour? | 600 mL/hour | Divide mock volume by the time over which it is removed. Here 1,800 mL ÷ 3 hours = 600 mL/hour. A calculated value is for verification within the trainee’s role. | Objective 3. [UK Kidney Association · Fluid management](https://guidelines.ukkidney.org/haemodialysis/rationale-for-clinical-practice-guidelines/fluid-in-haemodialysis/) |
| 16 | A patient’s pre-dialysis weight is 72.0 kg and the target weight is 70.0 kg. The unit counts 400 mL for rinse-back and drinks. The session is 4 hours. What is the hourly ultrafiltration rate? | 600 mL per hour | 2,000 mL plus 400 mL is 2,400 mL, and 2,400 divided by 4 hours is 600 mL per hour. |  |
| 17 | Two patients each have 3,200 mL to remove in 4 hours. One weighs 50 kg and the other 80 kg. Which statement is correct? | The 50 kg patient has the higher rate per kg, 16 mL per kg per hour | 800 divided by 50 is 16 mL per kg per hour, against 10 for the 80 kg patient. |  |
| 18 | The order says blood flow 300 mL per minute, but the chart shows the pump ran at 250 all session. Who decides whether the prescription changes? | The nephrologist, after the nurse is told | The technician records what was delivered and reports it; the prescription belongs to the nephrologist. |  |
| 19 | A patient loses 15 minutes at each of 13 sessions in a month. How much treatment time is lost? | 3 hours 15 minutes | 15 times 13 is 195 minutes, which is 3 hours 15 minutes. |  |
| 20 | Why is the blood pump slowed briefly before the post-dialysis BUN sample is drawn? | To clear recirculated, already cleaned blood from the arterial line | A sample containing recirculated blood has a falsely low BUN and makes the session look better than it was. |  |
| 21 | Pre-dialysis BUN is 80 mg/dL and post-dialysis BUN is 30 mg/dL. What is the URR, and how does it compare with the commonly cited minimum? | 62.5 percent, below the 65 percent minimum | (80 minus 30) divided by 80 is 62.5 percent. |  |
| 22 | A late patient’s session is cut from 4 hours to 3, and the 3,000 mL goal is unchanged. What should the technician do? | Tell the nurse that the rate would rise from 750 to 1,000 mL per hour before the machine is set | The hourly rate has risen without a decision; the nurse must confirm the goal. |  |

## Module 5: Dialysis Infection Prevention and Waste Management

| Q | Stem | Keyed answer | Explanation | Objective |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | To whom do Standard Precautions apply in the dialysis unit? | Every patient, with precautions matched to the task | Use Standard Precautions for every patient. Select protective measures according to the anticipated exposure during the task. | Objective 1. [CDC · Infection prevention in chronic hemodialysis](https://www.cdc.gov/mmwr/preview/mmwrhtml/rr5005a1.htm) |
| 2 | What should you do after removing used gloves? | Perform hand hygiene | Gloves can have small defects and hands can become contaminated during removal. Hand hygiene is needed after removal. | Objective 1. [CDC · Core interventions for dialysis bloodstream infection prevention](https://www.cdc.gov/dialysis-safety/hcp/clinical-safety/) |
| 3 | A task may splash blood toward your face. Which PPE addresses that specific risk? | Eye protection with a mask, or an appropriate face shield, according to the task and facility policy | Face and eye protection help prevent a splash from reaching mucous membranes. Use gloves and protective clothing when the task also exposes hands or clothing. | Objective 1. [CDC · Infection prevention in chronic hemodialysis](https://www.cdc.gov/mmwr/preview/mmwrhtml/rr5005a1.htm) |
| 4 | An observer records 20 hand hygiene opportunities but sees the action performed 15 times. What can this observation support? | Targeted feedback on missed opportunities and a follow-up observation | A process audit shows which safety steps were performed during observed opportunities. Use the findings for feedback and improvement. | Objective 4. [CDC · Core interventions for dialysis bloodstream infection prevention](https://www.cdc.gov/dialysis-safety/hcp/clinical-safety/) |
| 5 | You handled used tubing with gloves and are now asked to get clean gauze from a common supply cart. What do you do? | Remove the used gloves, perform hand hygiene and retrieve supplies using the clean-supply procedure | Hands and gloves can carry contamination to clean stock. Remove the gloves and perform hand hygiene before entering the clean workflow. | Objective 1. [CDC · Infection prevention in chronic hemodialysis](https://www.cdc.gov/mmwr/preview/mmwrhtml/rr5005a1.htm) |
| 6 | A patient is scheduled under the unit’s hepatitis B isolation protocol, but the station assignment includes equipment also used for other patients. What should you do? | Pause the affected setup and alert the charge nurse so the approved precautions can be verified | Dialysis-specific precautions for hepatitis B can include assigned equipment and other controls. The charge nurse verifies the correct arrangement under the facility protocol. | Objective 4. [CDC · Infection prevention in chronic hemodialysis](https://www.cdc.gov/mmwr/preview/mmwrhtml/rr5005a1.htm) |
| 7 | Which source sets the required wet contact time for an approved surface disinfectant? | The disinfectant’s instructions for use, applied through the facility procedure | The product instructions specify how long the surface must remain wet. Follow the facility-approved process for that product. | Objective 2. [CDC · Dialysis station disinfection checklist](https://www.cdc.gov/dialysis-safety/media/pdfs/env-checklist-508.pdf) |
| 8 | A cleaned chair looks dry, but the surface dried before the required disinfectant contact time was reached. How should you interpret its status? | Disinfection is incomplete until the required contact time has been achieved under the approved procedure | A dry appearance alone does not establish that the product had the required wet contact time. Complete the approved procedure before release. | Objective 2. [CDC · Dialysis station disinfection checklist](https://www.cdc.gov/dialysis-safety/media/pdfs/env-checklist-508.pdf) |
| 9 | The prior patient is still at the station after treatment and requires assistance before leaving. What does this mean for routine station disinfection? | Wait until the patient can safely leave, then complete routine station disinfection | Routine disinfection begins after the station is vacated. Patient safety takes priority when the person cannot yet move safely. | Objective 2. [CDC · Dialysis station disinfection checklist](https://www.cdc.gov/dialysis-safety/media/pdfs/env-checklist-508.pdf) |
| 10 | A used blood pressure cuff is about to be moved to a clean station, and the receiving chair is still within the required wet contact time. What should you do? | Keep the cuff in the used workflow for proper cleaning and disinfection, and keep the chair on hold until turnover is complete | The cuff and receiving chair each need their own completed process. Release each for reuse only after the facility’s applicable cleaning and disinfection requirements are met. | Objective 2. [CDC · Dialysis station disinfection checklist](https://www.cdc.gov/dialysis-safety/media/pdfs/env-checklist-508.pdf) |
| 11 | A used cuff was briefly placed on a clean supply cart before you noticed. What is the best response? | Stop use of the affected cart area and supplies, separate the cuff, and notify the responsible staff for assessment and remediation | Treat the contact as a clean-workflow breach. Prevent further use of the affected items and follow the facility’s escalation and remediation process. | Objective 4. [CDC · Infection prevention in chronic hemodialysis](https://www.cdc.gov/mmwr/preview/mmwrhtml/rr5005a1.htm) |
| 12 | Where should a used needle be discarded? | A designated puncture-resistant sharps container at the point of use | Point-of-use disposal in a designated sharps container reduces handling and protects clinical and waste personnel. | Objective 3. [Philippine DOH · Health Care Waste Management Manual](https://ntp.doh.gov.ph/download/health-care-waste-management-manual/) |
| 13 | The sharps container has reached its marked fill line. What does this indicate? | It is due for closure and replacement using the facility procedure | The fill line identifies when safe capacity has been reached. Close and replace the container through the approved process to limit protrusions and needlestick injuries. | Objective 3. [Philippine DOH · Health Care Waste Management Manual](https://ntp.doh.gov.ph/download/health-care-waste-management-manual/) |
| 14 | A used sharp punctures your skin during turnover. What should you do promptly? | Wash the puncture with soap and water, immediately report it, and seek evaluation through the facility’s exposure pathway | Prompt first aid and reporting support timely assessment for bloodborne infections and any indicated follow-up. | Objective 3. [CDC/NIOSH · Bloodborne infectious disease and exposure response](https://www.cdc.gov/niosh/topics/bbp/default.html) |
| 15 | While a trainee removes used supplies, blood splashes into their eye. What is the appropriate immediate response? | Flush the eye with clean water or saline, then immediately report the exposure and obtain evaluation | Irrigation is the appropriate first aid for an eye exposure. Prompt reporting and clinical evaluation are needed even without symptoms. | Objective 3. [CDC/NIOSH · Bloodborne infectious disease and exposure response](https://www.cdc.gov/niosh/topics/bbp/default.html) |
| 16 | When removing protective equipment after turnover, which item comes off first? | Gloves | Gloves are the most contaminated item and are removed first; hand hygiene is always the final step. |  |
| 17 | You silence an alarm by touching the machine screen but do not touch the patient. Is hand hygiene needed before you go to another station? | Yes, the station’s surfaces count as contaminated | Hands or gloves that touched one station can carry germs to the next. |  |
| 18 | A surface is wiped and then dried with a towel 20 seconds later. The label contact time is 3 minutes. What is the status of the surface? | Not disinfected; it must stay wet for the full contact time | Disinfectant works only while the surface is wet for the time on the label. |  |
| 19 | Unit policy calls for a 0.5 percent solution. The bottle is 5 percent. How much concentrate makes 2,000 mL? | 200 mL | (0.5 divided by 5) times 2,000 equals 200 mL, with 1,800 mL of water. |  |
| 20 | The label ratio 1:100 is read as 1 part in 100 parts total. How much concentrate is needed for 1,000 mL? | 10 mL | 1,000 divided by 100 is 10 mL, plus 990 mL of water. |  |
| 21 | According to CDC guidance, which patient requires a dedicated machine and a separate room or area? | A patient who is hepatitis B surface antigen positive | Hepatitis B survives on surfaces and spreads from tiny amounts of blood, so it alone requires isolation in that guidance. |  |
| 22 | After a needlestick you have washed the wound. Why must you report it during the same shift? | Because protective treatment, if needed, works best when started within hours | Speed of reporting is the part the exposed person controls. |  |

## Module 6: Machines, Dialyzers, and the Extracorporeal Circuit

| Q | Stem | Keyed answer | Explanation | Objective |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Which component propels blood through the extracorporeal circuit? | Blood pump | The blood pump moves blood through the extracorporeal bloodlines and dialyzer. | Objective 1. [NIDDK · Hemodialysis circuit](https://www.niddk.nih.gov/news/media-library/17961) |
| 2 | Where do blood and dialysate exchange small solutes across a membrane? | In the dialyzer | The dialyzer houses the membrane between the separate blood and dialysate pathways. | Objective 1. [NIDDK · Hemodialysis](https://www.niddk.nih.gov/health-information/kidney-disease/kidney-failure/hemodialysis) |
| 3 | A diagram labels one line ‘blood to patient.’ Which pathway is this on a typical two-line circuit? | Venous return line | In standard circuit nomenclature, the arterial line withdraws blood and the venous line returns blood to the patient. | Objective 1. [NIDDK · Hemodialysis circuit](https://www.niddk.nih.gov/news/media-library/17961) |
| 4 | How does the conventional circuit expose blood to dialysate for exchange while keeping the fluids separated? | They flow on opposite sides of a membrane. | The dialyzer membrane allows selective transport between separate fluid pathways. | Objective 1. [NIDDK · Hemodialysis](https://www.niddk.nih.gov/health-information/kidney-disease/kidney-failure/hemodialysis) |
| 5 | A detector monitors spent dialysate for evidence that blood crossed the membrane. Which safeguard is this? | Blood leak detector | The dialyzer blood leak detector monitors spent dialysate for blood. External leakage at a connection requires separate observation and response. | Objective 2. [FDA · Hemodialysis delivery systems](https://www.fda.gov/medical-devices/guidance-documents-medical-devices-and-radiation-emitting-products/guidance-content-premarket-notifications-hemodialysis-delivery-systems-guidance-cdrh-reviewers-and) |
| 6 | On a disconnected trainer, you are asked to identify the area where the machine monitors for air before blood would return to the patient. What do you trace? | The venous return pathway and the model’s air detector and clamp area | Trace the return path and identify the air detection and clamp functions on the actual model using its instructions. | Objective 2. [NIDDK · Hemodialysis circuit](https://www.niddk.nih.gov/news/media-library/17961) |
| 7 | What written reference determines how your station’s specific machine and compatible disposables are prepared? | The applicable model and disposable instructions for use, applied through facility procedure | Models and disposables differ. Use the applicable instructions and the facility’s validated pre-use process. | Objective 3. [Fresenius Medical Care · Examples of model-specific operator manuals](https://freseniusmedicalcare.com/en-us/support/product-support-documents/operators-manuals-hemodialysis-hd/) |
| 8 | What is a purpose of priming the extracorporeal circuit before use? | Prepare the fluid pathway and clear air as directed | Priming prepares the prescribed circuit for use and clears air as specified. Independent readiness checks are still required. | Objective 3. [Fresenius Medical Care · Example operator manual for the 2008T](https://www.freseniusmedicalcare.com/content/dam/fmcna/live/support/documents/operator%27s-manuals---hemodialysis-%28hd%29/2008t-operator%27s-manuals/490122_Rev_AB.pdf) |
| 9 | The machine self-test passed. One assigned independent readiness check is not yet documented as complete. What does this tell you? | Readiness remains unverified until all required checks are completed and documented. | A passed self-test covers its defined test functions. Confirm that assigned independent checks were completed before releasing the station. | Objective 3. [Fresenius Medical Care · Example operator manual for the 2008T](https://www.freseniusmedicalcare.com/content/dam/fmcna/live/support/documents/operator%27s-manuals---hemodialysis-%28hd%29/2008t-operator%27s-manuals/490122_Rev_AB.pdf) |
| 10 | The available bloodline set resembles the usual one, but compatibility with this machine is uncertain. What should you do? | Hold setup and verify compatibility using the current instructions and the supervising operator. | Appearance is insufficient to verify compatibility. Confirm the exact disposable and configuration before opening or using it. | Objective 3. [Fresenius Medical Care · Examples of model-specific operator manuals](https://freseniusmedicalcare.com/en-us/support/product-support-documents/operators-manuals-hemodialysis-hd/) |
| 11 | After a supervised setup, you notice visible air remains in the mock return line. What is your next step? | Keep the circuit out of use, alert the supervisor and complete the approved priming checks before release. | Visible air requires correction and verification under the model instructions before the circuit is considered ready. | Objective 3. [Fresenius Medical Care · Example operator manual for the 2008T](https://www.freseniusmedicalcare.com/content/dam/fmcna/live/support/documents/operator%27s-manuals---hemodialysis-%28hd%29/2008t-operator%27s-manuals/490122_Rev_AB.pdf) |
| 12 | What does the absence of a machine alarm establish about the access site and external connections? | It establishes only that no monitored alarm condition is currently being signaled. Direct observation remains necessary. | Alarms monitor defined conditions within their ranges and have detection limits. Keep the access and external connections visible and follow the authorized monitoring process. | Objective 2. [FDA · Hemodialysis delivery systems](https://www.fda.gov/medical-devices/guidance-documents-medical-devices-and-radiation-emitting-products/guidance-content-premarket-notifications-hemodialysis-delivery-systems-guidance-cdrh-reviewers-and) |
| 13 | Before the patient arrives, a required self-test fails and the failure remains unresolved after authorized checks. What should you do? | Keep the machine out of service, identify its status and notify the authorized nurse or equipment team through facility procedure. | A failed readiness check leaves equipment unverified. Prevent use and follow the local reporting and release procedure. | Objective 4. [Fresenius Medical Care · Example operator manual for the 2008T](https://www.freseniusmedicalcare.com/content/dam/fmcna/live/support/documents/operator%27s-manuals---hemodialysis-%28hd%29/2008t-operator%27s-manuals/490122_Rev_AB.pdf) |
| 14 | Which equipment log entry best supports follow-up after a failed pre-use self-test? | 07:20 · Station 4, machine asset 018 · Air detector self-test failed · Machine held from use · Charge nurse and biomedical team notified. | The entry identifies the equipment, exact observed failure, time, use status and notifications, so authorized staff can investigate and track follow-up. | Objective 4. [Fresenius Medical Care · Example operator manual for the 2008T](https://www.freseniusmedicalcare.com/content/dam/fmcna/live/support/documents/operator%27s-manuals---hemodialysis-%28hd%29/2008t-operator%27s-manuals/490122_Rev_AB.pdf) |
| 15 | During treatment you notice blood at an external connection, but the blood leak alarm has not activated. What should you do? | Immediately alert the nurse and follow the facility’s emergency response within your assigned role. | External blood loss can occur without triggering the dialyzer blood leak detector. Respond to the observed hazard promptly and within scope. | Objective 4. [FDA · Hemodialysis delivery systems](https://www.fda.gov/medical-devices/guidance-documents-medical-devices-and-radiation-emitting-products/guidance-content-premarket-notifications-hemodialysis-delivery-systems-guidance-cdrh-reviewers-and) |
| 16 | What is the main reason a dialyzer is primed from the bottom upward at a slow pump speed? | Rising fluid pushes the air out ahead of it and does not trap it in the fibers | Air trapped in fibers blocks them and lowers clearance. |  |
| 17 | A primed circuit shows pale streaks along the dialyzer. What do they most likely mean? | Air trapped in the fibers | Streaks are air-locked fibers; the dialyzer needs further priming or replacement by the unit’s procedure. |  |
| 18 | Which statement about the machine’s rinse program is correct? | It flushes fluid but does not disinfect | A rinse is never counted as disinfection. |  |
| 19 | After a chemical disinfection program, what must be shown before the machine is used? | A negative residual test with the correct strip | Only the residual test proves the chemical has been rinsed out. |  |
| 20 | When should the dialysate lines be connected to the dialyzer? | When the machine is out of bypass with conductivity and temperature in range | Dialysate that is out of range must not reach the dialyzer. |  |

## Module 7: Water Treatment, Concentrates, and Dialysate

| Q | Stem | Keyed answer | Explanation | Objective |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Which treatment component is designed to remove chlorine and chloramine before reverse osmosis? | Activated carbon stage | Activated carbon adsorbs or reduces disinfectant residues. The facility uses prescribed monitoring to detect breakthrough. | Objective 1. [CDC · Water systems in health-care facilities](https://www.cdc.gov/infection-control/hcp/environmental-control/water.html) |
| 2 | What is the stream of treated water that passes through the reverse osmosis (RO) membrane called? | Product water or permeate | Permeate is the product stream that passes through the RO membrane. The reject stream carries concentrated contaminants away. | Objective 1. [CDC · Water systems in health-care facilities](https://www.cdc.gov/infection-control/hcp/environmental-control/water.html) |
| 3 | Why can a clinic need dialysis-specific water treatment even when its source water is safe to drink? | Dialysis uses large volumes of water to make dialysate, so additional contaminant controls are needed. | Dialysis uses substantial water volumes and the membrane provides an exchange surface. Dialysis-specific chemical and microbiological controls apply to the water used to prepare dialysate. | Objective 1. [CDC · Water use in dialysis](https://www.cdc.gov/dialysis-safety/hcp/recommendations-resources/water-use-in-dialysis.html) |
| 4 | What is the main purpose of the dialysis-water distribution loop? | Carry treated product water to the points of use | The distribution system delivers treated product water to points of use. Its design and maintenance help control contamination. | Objective 1. [CDC · Water systems in health-care facilities](https://www.cdc.gov/infection-control/hcp/environmental-control/water.html) |
| 5 | On the facility’s approved diagram, you are asked to point to the route feeding product water to a station. Which path should you trace? | RO product outlet through the treated-water distribution system to the station feed | Product water travels from the RO product side through the facility’s distribution system to the station. Keep treated-water feeds distinct from drains and concentrate connections. | Objective 1. [CDC · Dialysis wall boxes and drains](https://www.cdc.gov/dialysis-safety/hcp/infection-control/wall-boxes-drains.html) |
| 6 | Which two tests measure different aspects of microbiological water quality? | Microbial culture and endotoxin testing | Culture looks for organisms recoverable under the specified culture conditions. Endotoxin testing measures a bacterial product and answers a different question. | Objective 2. [CDC · Water use in dialysis](https://www.cdc.gov/dialysis-safety/hcp/recommendations-resources/water-use-in-dialysis.html) |
| 7 | What does pH describe in a prepared dialysate sample? | How acidic or alkaline the sample is | pH measures acidity or alkalinity. Conductivity measures a property related to the dissolved ions that carry electrical current. | Objective 3. [Fresenius Medical Care · Model-specific machine operator’s manual, example](https://www.freseniusmedicalcare.com/content/dam/fmcna/live/support/documents/operator%27s-manuals---hemodialysis-%28hd%29/2008t-operator%27s-manuals/490122_Rev_AB.pdf) |
| 8 | The dialysate conductivity is within the expected range. What can you conclude from that result alone? | The measured conductivity is within range. Required identity checks and other tests still apply. | Conductivity describes the combined electrical behavior of dissolved ions. It cannot identify each electrolyte or establish microbiological quality. | Objective 3. [Fresenius Medical Care · Model-specific machine operator’s manual, example](https://www.freseniusmedicalcare.com/content/dam/fmcna/live/support/documents/operator%27s-manuals---hemodialysis-%28hd%29/2008t-operator%27s-manuals/490122_Rev_AB.pdf) |
| 9 | A total chlorine result and an endotoxin result both appear in a log. Why does the facility retain both? | They assess different hazards, so an acceptable result on one cannot clear the other. | Total chlorine helps assess disinfectant breakthrough. Endotoxin monitoring evaluates a different contaminant and remains necessary under the applicable plan. | Objective 2. [CDC · Water use in dialysis](https://www.cdc.gov/dialysis-safety/hcp/recommendations-resources/water-use-in-dialysis.html) |
| 10 | A concentrate container fits the connector but its label differs from the verified formulation. What should you do before station use? | Hold setup and ask the authorized team to verify and resolve the discrepancy. | Check the actual concentrate identity, compatibility and prescribed formulation. A compatible-looking connector or conductivity reading does not authorize a substitution. | Objective 3. [Fresenius Medical Care · Model-specific machine operator’s manual, example](https://www.freseniusmedicalcare.com/content/dam/fmcna/live/support/documents/operator%27s-manuals---hemodialysis-%28hd%29/2008t-operator%27s-manuals/490122_Rev_AB.pdf) |
| 11 | You are assigned to collect a mock RO-water sample, but the collection point on the form is unclear. What is the appropriate next step? | Verify the designated sampling point and collection method before sampling. | A result is interpretable only in relation to the correct sampling point, collection method and timing. Resolve missing or conflicting instructions before collecting. | Objective 2. [CDC · Water use in dialysis](https://www.cdc.gov/dialysis-safety/hcp/recommendations-resources/water-use-in-dialysis.html) |
| 12 | The final total chlorine entry shows ‘above action limit’ at the required sampling point before the first session. Which statement best describes the water status? | The affected supply has not met a required release check. Follow the site’s hold and escalation process. | The out-of-range result identifies an unresolved water-quality concern. Use the facility’s procedure to contain the affected supply and obtain authorized clearance. | Objective 4. [CDC · Water use in dialysis](https://www.cdc.gov/dialysis-safety/hcp/recommendations-resources/water-use-in-dialysis.html) |
| 13 | A total chlorine check is above the facility’s action limit and no patients have started. What is your first priority? | Prevent treatment from starting on the affected supply and promptly alert the authorized nurse and water-system lead according to facility policy. | Prevent exposure and promptly escalate. The authorized team determines repeat testing, corrective action, and clearance under site procedure. | Objective 4. [Philippine DOH · Dialysis facility standards](https://elibrary.judiciary.gov.ph/thebookshelf/showdocs/10/50687) |
| 14 | Which log entry best supports follow-up for that out-of-range result? | 06:35 · Designated sampling port · Total chlorine above the site limit · Actual reading and units recorded · Affected stations held · Charge nurse and water-system lead notified. | Capture the observation, time, exact result and units, affected use, notifications and follow-up status. Preserve the original result and link any subsequent checks. | Objective 4. [CDC · Water use in dialysis](https://www.cdc.gov/dialysis-safety/hcp/recommendations-resources/water-use-in-dialysis.html) |
| 15 | An authorized staff member obtains a normal repeat result after the hold. What should you do before telling staff the loop is ready? | Confirm documented release by the designated authority under the facility procedure. | A normal repeat is part of the investigation. Return to use follows the facility’s clearance and documentation requirements. | Objective 4. [Philippine DOH · Dialysis facility standards](https://elibrary.judiciary.gov.ph/thebookshelf/showdocs/10/50687) |
| 16 | Before a total chlorine sample is taken at the start of the day, the water system should have been running for at least how long? | 15 minutes | Flowing water shows how the carbon performs under working conditions; water that sat overnight reads falsely low. |  |
| 17 | Which widely used limit applies to total chlorine in water used for dialysis? | At most 0.1 mg/L | The widely used standard limit is 0.1 mg/L; drinking-water levels are far too high for dialysis. |  |
| 18 | Why is the hardness test commonly done at the end of the treatment day? | The softener is most used up then, so a failure is most likely to show | Testing at the point of greatest exhaustion gives the earliest warning. |  |
| 19 | A bicarbonate container is one third full of solution mixed earlier. A coworker suggests adding freshly mixed solution to fill it. What is correct? | Never top up; use a clean container for the fresh batch and discard the old solution at its time limit | Topping up carries bacteria into the new batch and makes the mixing time on the label false. |  |
| 20 | Within ten minutes, three patients on the same shift report chest pain and shortness of breath, and the blood in their lines looks unusually clear and red. What should the technician suspect and do first? | A water or dialysate problem; call the nurse at once and say that several patients are affected | The same complaint in several patients at once points to what they share: the water and dialysate. |  |
| 21 | Total chlorine after the first carbon tank is 0.3 mg/L and after the second tank is below 0.1 mg/L. Who decides whether treatments may start? | The person the unit authorizes, after the technician reports both results | The technician holds, reports exact results, and records the decision; release belongs to the authorized person. |  |
| 22 | The acid concentrate label shows a different potassium value from the prescription, but machine conductivity is in range. What does this mean? | Conductivity cannot detect the difference; hold the setup and tell the nurse | Small differences in potassium or calcium do not move conductivity out of range. |  |

## Module 8: Vascular Access Recognition and Protection

| Q | Stem | Keyed answer | Explanation | Objective |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | What is an arteriovenous (AV) fistula? | A surgically created direct connection between an artery and a vein | An AV fistula connects the patient’s artery and vein directly to create access for hemodialysis. | Objective 1. [NIDDK · Hemodialysis and vascular access](https://www.niddk.nih.gov/health-information/kidney-disease/kidney-failure/hemodialysis) |
| 2 | What distinguishes an AV graft from a typical AV fistula? | It connects an artery and vein using an implanted conduit. | A graft uses an implanted conduit, typically synthetic, to bridge the artery and vein. A fistula joins the vessels directly. | Objective 1. [NIDDK · Hemodialysis and vascular access](https://www.niddk.nih.gov/health-information/kidney-disease/kidney-failure/hemodialysis) |
| 3 | Where does a hemodialysis central venous catheter (CVC) provide access? | In a large central vein | A CVC is a tube placed into a large central vein to withdraw and return blood for hemodialysis. | Objective 1. [NIDDK · Hemodialysis and vascular access](https://www.niddk.nih.gov/health-information/kidney-disease/kidney-failure/hemodialysis) |
| 4 | Why is access type relevant when discussing bloodstream infection risk? | Catheters generally carry higher bloodstream infection risk than fistulas or grafts, while all require infection prevention. | Bloodstream infection risk varies by access type. Central venous catheters carry the highest relative risk, and infection precautions apply to every type. | Objective 1. [CDC · Dialysis access and infections](https://www.cdc.gov/dialysis-safety/about/index.html) |
| 5 | On a teaching model you see a skin exit site with external catheter lumens. Which access does this best represent? | Hemodialysis CVC | An external exit site and catheter lumens are consistent with a CVC model. Verify a patient’s actual access using the chart and the responsible clinician. | Objective 1. [NIDDK · Hemodialysis and vascular access](https://www.niddk.nih.gov/health-information/kidney-disease/kidney-failure/hemodialysis) |
| 6 | What is the term for the vibration that can be felt over a functioning fistula or graft? | Thrill | A thrill is a palpable vibration from blood flow. A bruit is the sound heard during authorized assessment. | Objective 2. [National Kidney Foundation · Hemodialysis access](https://www.kidney.org/kidney-topics/hemodialysis-access) |
| 7 | A patient asks why staff should avoid routine blood pressure measurements on the access arm. What is the best explanation? | A cuff can compress the access. Use the site directed by the care team. | Avoid routine compression of the access arm and confirm the appropriate alternate measurement site with the clinical team. | Objective 2. [National Kidney Foundation · Hemodialysis access](https://www.kidney.org/kidney-topics/hemodialysis-access) |
| 8 | During treatment a patient wants to cover the access to stay warm. How can you help? | Keep the access and connections visible and offer to place the blanket away from them. | Keeping the access and connections visible supports early recognition of bleeding or dislodgement. Offer warmth without blocking observation. | Objective 2. [National Kidney Foundation · Hemodialysis access](https://www.kidney.org/kidney-topics/hemodialysis-access) |
| 9 | You notice a CVC dressing is wet and partly loose before treatment. What should you do? | Protect the area from further handling and promptly report it to the nurse for authorized care. | A wet or loose catheter dressing needs prompt review. The trainee should avoid unassigned dressing or line manipulation. | Objective 2. [CDC · Dialysis access and infections](https://www.cdc.gov/dialysis-safety/about/index.html) |
| 10 | A patient says their usual fistula vibration was present yesterday and is absent this morning. What does this finding suggest? | It may indicate access flow dysfunction and needs prompt clinical assessment. | An absent or newly changed thrill can reflect impaired flow and is a warning sign. Clinical assessment determines the cause and readiness for use. | Objective 3. [KDOQI · Vascular access guideline, Table 13.2](https://www.kidney.org/sites/default/files/2026-07/2019-kdoqi-guideline-vascular-access.pdf) |
| 11 | Which combination warrants concern for a possible access infection? | New site redness, warmth and drainage | New local inflammation or drainage may indicate infection. Report objective findings, including any fever or chills reported by the patient. | Objective 3. [CDC · Dialysis access and infections](https://www.cdc.gov/dialysis-safety/about/index.html) |
| 12 | The patient reports a newly absent thrill just before treatment. What is your first action? | Pause access use and promptly notify the nurse for assessment. | A change in thrill needs assessment before use. The supervising clinician decides the next step. | Objective 4. [KDOQI · Vascular access guideline, Table 13.2](https://www.kidney.org/sites/default/files/2026-07/2019-kdoqi-guideline-vascular-access.pdf) |
| 13 | During treatment, new swelling and pain develop near an AV access needle. What should you do? | Alert the nurse promptly, describe what you observed and follow the unit response within your role. | Swelling and pain can indicate infiltration or another access problem. The nurse must assess promptly, and trainees should avoid unassigned manipulation. | Objective 4. [KDOQI · Vascular access guideline, Table 13.2](https://www.kidney.org/sites/default/files/2026-07/2019-kdoqi-guideline-vascular-access.pdf) |
| 14 | You see heavy bleeding from an access site. What is the best response? | Activate the unit’s emergency response immediately and assist under the emergency procedure within your training. | Heavy access bleeding can become life threatening. Summon help immediately and support the authorized response. | Objective 4. [National Kidney Foundation · Hemodialysis access](https://www.kidney.org/kidney-topics/hemodialysis-access) |
| 15 | Which handoff most accurately reports the opening-case concern? | Before treatment, the patient reported that their usual fistula vibration was absent this morning. Access use is on hold pending nursing assessment. | A useful handoff distinguishes the patient report from a clinical diagnosis and states timing, the hold, and who needs to assess next. | Objective 4. [Philippine DOH · Dialysis facility standards](https://elibrary.judiciary.gov.ph/thebookshelf/showdocs/10/50687) |
| 16 | During the pre-treatment check you hear a high-pitched, whistling bruit over a fistula that usually has a low whoosh. What should you do? | Tell the nurse before the access is used, because it may point to a narrowing | A change to a high-pitched or whistling sound can mean stenosis and is reported before treatment. |  |
| 17 | Why is tape never wrapped in a full circle around the access arm? | It can act like a tourniquet and slow blood flow through the access | Anything that squeezes the arm slows flow, and slow flow can clot the access. |  |
| 18 | A catheter patient arrives with one cap missing from a lumen. What is the correct response? | Call the nurse immediately, stay with the patient, and do not handle the catheter unless directed | An uncapped lumen risks air entry, bleeding, and infection, and catheter handling is a nursing task. |  |
| 19 | When holding pressure after needle removal, how firm should the pressure be? | Firm enough to stop bleeding while a thrill can still be felt above or below the fingers | Pressure that stops all flow can clot the access; peeking or pressing on a needle still in the vessel causes harm. |  |
| 20 | During treatment a patient reports burning and you see a firm swelling at the venous needle. What is this most likely, and what do you do? | Infiltration; call the nurse now and keep the arm still and in view | Swelling and pain at a needle suggest blood entering the tissue, which the nurse must assess at once. |  |
| 21 | In your report of an absent thrill, why do you include when the patient last felt the usual buzz? | It tells the team how old a possible clot could be | The last known normal time helps the team judge how quickly it must act. |  |

## Module 9: The Hemodialysis Session

| Q | Stem | Keyed answer | Explanation | Objective |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Which is an appropriate pair for verifying identity before dialysis when approved by the facility? | Full name and date of birth | Use two person-specific identifiers and match them to the order and care record. The station and schedule identify a location and time. | Objective 1. [Joint Commission · Two patient identifiers](https://www.jointcommission.org/en-us/knowledge-library/support-center/standards-interpretation/standards-faqs/000001463) |
| 2 | Which source defines the treatment parameters to be verified before the session? | The current authorized patient-specific order | The current order is the source of the prescribed treatment. Verify any discrepancy through the authorized clinician. | Objective 1. [Philippine DOH · Dialysis facility standards](https://elibrary.judiciary.gov.ph/thebookshelf/showdocs/10/50687) |
| 3 | Today’s repeated pre-treatment weight differs markedly from the patient’s recent trend. What does a consistent repeat measurement establish? | It verifies the measurement under the conditions checked. The discrepancy still needs review. | Confirm scale, units, patient identity and measurement conditions as assigned. The verified current weight is a data point for clinical review, not a new prescription. | Objective 1. [UK Kidney Association · Fluid assessment and management](https://guidelines.ukkidney.org/haemodialysis/rationale-for-clinical-practice-guidelines/fluid-in-haemodialysis/) |
| 4 | You confirm a verified pre-treatment weight remains unexpectedly high. What should you do before applying treatment settings? | Report the verified weight and comparison to the nurse. Hold any setting that depends on the unresolved discrepancy. | The nurse or authorized clinician reviews the unexpected result and confirms the treatment plan. A technician reports the measurement and avoids independent changes. | Objective 1. [UK Kidney Association · Fluid assessment and management](https://guidelines.ukkidney.org/haemodialysis/rationale-for-clinical-practice-guidelines/fluid-in-haemodialysis/) |
| 5 | A medication listed on a previous record is absent from today’s order. Someone asks you to prepare it as usual. What should you do? | Ask the authorized nurse to verify the order and handle it within facility policy. | Medication preparation, administration and changes depend on a verified order, legal scope and facility authorization. | Objective 1. [Philippine DOH · Dialysis facility standards](https://elibrary.judiciary.gov.ph/thebookshelf/showdocs/10/50687) |
| 6 | What is the term for observations made while the dialysis treatment is running? | Intradialytic observations | Intradialytic observations are made during treatment, such as assigned vital signs, symptoms and equipment or access observations. | Objective 2. [NIDDK · Hemodialysis](https://www.niddk.nih.gov/health-information/kidney-disease/kidney-failure/hemodialysis) |
| 7 | Why record the actual time of an assigned vital sign and the treatment phase? | So the team can relate the value to symptoms, interventions and treatment progression | Timing gives the value clinical context and supports comparison across the treatment record. | Objective 2. [Philippine DOH · Dialysis facility standards](https://elibrary.judiciary.gov.ph/thebookshelf/showdocs/10/50687) |
| 8 | The machine has not alarmed. The patient reports dizziness and appears pale. Which interpretation is most appropriate? | Observed symptoms warrant prompt clinical review regardless of the alarm status. | Machine alarms respond to monitored device conditions. They cannot replace patient observation or timely reporting of symptoms. | Objective 2. [UK Kidney Association · Fluid assessment and management](https://guidelines.ukkidney.org/haemodialysis/rationale-for-clinical-practice-guidelines/fluid-in-haemodialysis/) |
| 9 | A patient becomes lightheaded and an assigned blood pressure reading is markedly lower than the initial value. What should you do? | Notify the nurse promptly, report the symptoms, reading and time, and assist within the unit response procedure. | Symptoms with a substantial blood pressure change warrant prompt nursing assessment. Relay facts and follow authorized instructions. | Objective 2. [UK Kidney Association · Fluid assessment and management](https://guidelines.ukkidney.org/haemodialysis/rationale-for-clinical-practice-guidelines/fluid-in-haemodialysis/) |
| 10 | You are authorized to assist with specimen checks. A specimen label has the correct name but a different record number. What should you do? | Hold the specimen and alert the responsible staff to reconcile it under the approved process. | A mismatched identifier makes the specimen identity uncertain. Reconcile it through policy before sending or relying on it. | Objective 2. [WHO · Guidelines on drawing blood](https://www.who.int/publications-detail-redirect/9789241599221) |
| 11 | Which measurements should be distinguished when documenting a session? | Measured pre- and post-treatment weights and the prescribed target weight | The pre- and post-treatment weights are measurements. The target is a clinical goal and should remain identifiable as such. | Objective 3. [UK Kidney Association · Fluid assessment and management](https://guidelines.ukkidney.org/haemodialysis/rationale-for-clinical-practice-guidelines/fluid-in-haemodialysis/) |
| 12 | A session ended early. Why should the record show the actual treatment duration and the reason reported to the nurse? | The care team needs to assess delivered treatment and any follow-up rather than assume the full session occurred. | The difference between planned and delivered treatment is relevant to clinical review, continuity and documentation. | Objective 4. [Philippine DOH · Dialysis facility standards](https://elibrary.judiciary.gov.ph/thebookshelf/showdocs/10/50687) |
| 13 | The patient has left and visible soil was removed. The routine disinfectant wet contact time is still underway. Is the station ready? | The station remains on hold until routine turnover, including the required disinfection process, is complete. | The station is released only after the approved cleaning and disinfection requirements are completed. | Objective 3. [CDC · Dialysis station routine disinfection](https://www.cdc.gov/dialysis-safety/media/pdfs/env-checklist-508.pdf) |
| 14 | After disconnecting, the patient reports dizziness while seated. The next patient has arrived. What should you do? | Alert the nurse and wait for clinical direction before moving the patient or releasing the station. | A new symptom requires nursing assessment. Patient safety takes priority and routine turnover waits until the station is vacated safely. | Objective 3. [CDC · Dialysis station routine disinfection](https://www.cdc.gov/dialysis-safety/media/pdfs/env-checklist-508.pdf) |
| 15 | Which end-of-treatment handoff is most useful? | Treatment ended early at 10:40. The patient reported dizziness at 10:32. The nurse was notified, assessed the patient and requested a repeat observation that remains pending. | State what happened, when, who responded and what remains outstanding. The receiving clinician can confirm the pending action. | Objective 4. [AHRQ · SBAR](https://www.ahrq.gov/teamstepps-program/curriculum/communication/tools/sbar.html) |
| 16 | An alarm sounds. What do you look at first? | The patient | Patient first, access and lines second, screen third. |  |
| 17 | The venous pressure reading drops suddenly during treatment. What must you do at once? | Uncover and look at the access and the venous line | A fall in venous pressure can mean the line has separated or the needle has come out. |  |
| 18 | The air detector alarms and the venous clamp closes. Which action is correct? | Leave the clamp closed and call the nurse | The clamp is what protects the patient from air. |  |
| 19 | Three machines alarm for low water supply at the same time. What does that suggest? | A problem in the water system | One machine or several is the question that separates a machine fault from a supply fault. |  |
| 20 | A venous pressure alarm keeps repeating. A colleague suggests widening the limits. Why is that unsafe? | The machine could no longer detect the change it is warning about | Limits are a protection; the cause of the alarm has to be found. |  |

## Module 10: Complications and Emergency Response

| Q | Stem | Keyed answer | Explanation | Objective |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | What does intradialytic hypotension describe? | A fall to low blood pressure during dialysis | Intradialytic hypotension is low blood pressure during dialysis. A symptomatic fall needs prompt clinical review under the facility’s procedure. | Objective 1. [UK Kidney Association · Fluid assessment and management](https://guidelines.ukkidney.org/haemodialysis/rationale-for-clinical-practice-guidelines/fluid-in-haemodialysis/) |
| 2 | Which observation is a vascular access emergency? | Heavy bleeding from the access | Heavy access bleeding can be life-threatening. Initiate the unit’s emergency escalation immediately. | Objective 1. [National Kidney Foundation · Hemodialysis access](https://www.kidney.org/kidney-topics/hemodialysis-access) |
| 3 | The machine has no active alarm, but a patient reports new chest pain and difficulty breathing. What does the quiet alarm panel tell you? | It does not rule out a patient emergency. The symptoms warrant an immediate response. | A machine monitors defined equipment conditions. New chest pain and breathlessness require immediate clinical attention whether or not the machine signals an alarm. | Objective 1. [National Kidney Foundation · Hemodialysis treatment day checklist](https://www.kidney.org/sites/default/files/2026-09/441-0518_2608-patflyer_checklist_hemodialysis-v2.pdf) |
| 4 | A patient becomes lightheaded and develops cramps. An assigned measurement shows a major blood pressure drop from baseline. Which concern should you report? | Possible symptomatic intradialytic hypotension requiring prompt nursing assessment | Report the symptoms, pressure, timing and trend. The supervising clinician assesses the cause and directs treatment. | Objective 1. [UK Kidney Association · Fluid assessment and management](https://guidelines.ukkidney.org/haemodialysis/rationale-for-clinical-practice-guidelines/fluid-in-haemodialysis/) |
| 5 | A patient with a dialysis catheter has new chills and drainage near the exit site. Which concern should you raise? | Possible infection that needs prompt clinical review. | Chills and new exit-site drainage can accompany an access infection. Report both promptly without assigning a diagnosis. | Objective 1. [National Kidney Foundation · Hemodialysis access](https://www.kidney.org/kidney-topics/hemodialysis-access) |
| 6 | You find that a venous needle has moved and the patient is bleeding. No alarm is sounding. What should you do? | Call for urgent help immediately and activate the facility response within your assigned role. | Dislodgement can cause rapid blood loss. Call for help based on the observed hazard and assist under the emergency procedure within your training. | Objective 2. [National Kidney Foundation · Hemodialysis treatment day checklist](https://www.kidney.org/sites/default/files/2026-09/441-0518_2608-patflyer_checklist_hemodialysis-v2.pdf) |
| 7 | What is the purpose of a TeamSTEPPS call-out? | To communicate urgent information aloud so the team can respond | A call-out shares critical information quickly with the team at the point of care. Include the location and immediate concern. | Objective 2. [AHRQ · TeamSTEPPS call-out](https://www.ahrq.gov/teamstepps-program/curriculum/communication/tools/callout.html) |
| 8 | What is a check-back? | Repeating a received instruction and having the sender confirm it | The receiver repeats the instruction, and the sender confirms or corrects it. This closes the communication loop. | Objective 3. [AHRQ · TeamSTEPPS tools](https://www.ahrq.gov/teamstepps-program/resources/modules/index.html) |
| 9 | Which initial call-out is most useful in the opening case? | Urgent help at station 4. The patient has new chest pain and trouble breathing. | A concise call-out states the urgency, location and observed problem so the team can act quickly. | Objective 2. [AHRQ · TeamSTEPPS call-out](https://www.ahrq.gov/teamstepps-program/curriculum/communication/tools/callout.html) |
| 10 | The nurse assigns you to call the emergency team while another colleague stays at the station. What should you do? | Confirm the instruction, activate the emergency response as directed and report back. | Acknowledge and complete the assigned task promptly. Closing the loop lets the team know it has been completed. | Objective 3. [AHRQ · TeamSTEPPS tools](https://www.ahrq.gov/teamstepps-program/resources/modules/index.html) |
| 11 | Once immediate care is underway, which documentation entry best supports handoff and review? | 10:18 · Patient reported new chest pain and difficulty breathing · Charge nurse notified immediately · Emergency response activated at 10:19 · Assigned task and response documented. | Record the chronology, symptoms, escalation, assigned actions and follow-up objectively after urgent care needs are addressed. | Objective 3. [Philippine DOH · Dialysis facility standards](https://elibrary.judiciary.gov.ph/thebookshelf/showdocs/10/50687) |
| 12 | The emergency drill exposed confusion over who calls for help. What should your team contribute during debrief? | Describe what happened, clarify the role assignment and agree on one improvement to test. | Debrief uses observed events to identify what worked and what the team can practice or improve before the next drill. | Objective 3. [AHRQ · TeamSTEPPS tools](https://www.ahrq.gov/teamstepps-program/resources/modules/index.html) |
| 13 | Several stations on one water loop show an unexpected interruption. Why does the nurse or lead need to know which stations and patients are affected? | It helps the team identify the shared hazard, prioritize active patients and coordinate the contingency plan. | The scope of a disruption informs both clinical priorities and technical containment. Report affected stations and whether treatments are active. | Objective 4. [CDC · Dialysis water use and supply disruptions](https://www.cdc.gov/dialysis-safety/hcp/recommendations-resources/water-use-in-dialysis.html) |
| 14 | A power interruption occurs while multiple patients are receiving treatment.
