| 5 | Mang Pedring has a painless groin bulge that grows with fills and goes back when he lies down. This is most likely: | A hernia; report for review and teach the warning signs | It becomes urgent if painful, firm, red or accompanied by vomiting. |  |
| 6 | A painful, firm, red hernia with vomiting requires: | Emergency evaluation for possible strangulation | Bowel trapped in a hernia can lose its blood supply. |  |
| 7 | Mrs. Linda’s gauze soaks with clear fluid after each fill, nine days after an urgent-start catheter. This suggests: | A pericatheter leak; same-day report | The tunnel has not sealed. The team decides on smaller fills, position or a pause. |  |
| 8 | Uneven, doughy abdominal wall swelling with falling UF suggests: | An abdominal wall leak | Fluid may be dissecting into the tissue planes of the abdominal wall. |  |
| 9 | Which three details matter most after a power outage during APD? | Connection status, therapy delivered or missed, and symptoms, plus power status and the backup plan | These tell the team what was lost and whether the backup plan is safe to use. |  |
| 10 | Pain in the last minutes of drain is usually due to: | The catheter tip against bowel or bladder as fluid empties | Report it; the team may consider tidal APD. |  |
| 11 | When may backup CAPD be used after an outage? | Only with a written backup plan, supplies and validated competence | Backup is planned and validated in advance. |  |
| 12 | Blood-tinged effluent with dizziness and falling blood pressure requires: | Emergency evaluation | Bleeding with signs of low blood volume is an emergency. |  |
| 13 | Why does a full abdomen raise the risk of leaks and hernias? | Pressure inside the abdomen pushes fluid toward weak points, more so when sitting, straining or lifting | That is why lifting heavy loads, such as rice sacks, matters. |  |
| 14 | A long-term former PD patient has recurrent bowel obstruction symptoms and weight loss. What should be considered and reported? | Encapsulating peritoneal sclerosis | This rare late complication can appear even after PD has stopped. |  |
| 15 | Which item belongs in a typhoon go-bag for a PD patient? | Solutions, connection supplies, caps, medicines, records and the current order | With a go-bag, PD can continue in an evacuation center. |  |

## Module 9: Teach, Validate and Retrain

| Q | Stem | Keyed answer | Explanation | Objective |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | What evidence best supports independent competence? | Correct return demonstration without prompts, including critical steps and responses to common problems | Readiness is shown, not stated. Attendance and signatures do not show performance. |  |
| 2 | Which question is a true teach-back? | “Tell me in your own words what you would do if the bag is cloudy.” | Teach-back asks the learner to explain in their own words. Yes-or-no questions invite a polite yes. |  |
| 3 | How does the 2025 ISPD teaching position paper characterize its evidence? | Practice points, acknowledging a lack of high-certainty evidence | It is expert consensus. Use it, and describe it honestly. |  |
| 4 | In PDOPPS, facility training of 6 or more days was: | Associated with lower facility peritonitis risk (rate ratio 0.81) | Observational and facility-level. It may reflect better-resourced programs. |  |
| 5 | A caregiver completes a setup correctly only after reminders about the connector. Best action? | Reinforce, demonstrate the critical step, and schedule another unprompted check before assigning the task at home | A critical step that needs prompting is not yet independent. |  |
| 6 | Mrs. Corazon’s son will replace his sister as her caregiver. What is needed? | Assess his needs, train him for his role, observe his performance and document readiness before his sister leaves | A change of caregiver is a retraining trigger. Watching is not the same as doing. |  |
| 7 | Which is a retraining trigger? | Peritonitis | Peritonitis, exit-site infection, contamination, drift, a device or caregiver change and hospitalization all trigger reassessment. |  |
| 8 | Which training method best fits adult learners? | Short, purpose-linked sessions with demonstration and practice | Adults learn best in short sessions tied to what they want, with practice and coaching. |  |
| 9 | Why should training cover problems, not only routine steps? | Because safe home care depends on recognizing and responding to cloudy fluid, contamination and poor drain | The routine keeps the patient well on good days. Problem responses keep them safe on bad days. |  |
| 10 | Mang Fred nods at every explanation but cannot find the strength on the bag. A suitable adaptation is: | A picture checklist and color-coded strength stickers, verified by watching him | Adapt the method to the learner, then confirm by observation. |  |
| 11 | Why make mistakes in training feel safe? | So learners hide errors less at home | A learner afraid of blame hides contamination. Hidden contamination becomes peritonitis. |  |
| 12 | Who should be trained for a task? | The person who will actually perform it at home | Train the person who does the task, and plan a backup. |  |
| 13 | Which element belongs in readiness documentation? | Performed steps, cues needed, gaps, supports and the reassessment date | The next nurse needs to know what was seen, what helped and when to check again. |  |
| 14 | Mr. Arturo has had a year without peritonitis but now rests the cap on the table during fills. What is appropriate? | Name the drift without scolding, retrain the step and book a recheck | Drift is normal after months of success. Catching and correcting it is part of the job. |  |
| 15 | What does health literacy practice require? | Plain language, pictures, simple numbers, the person’s own language and checking understanding | Meet the learner where they are, and check by watching. |  |

## Module 10: Longitudinal Care, Nutrition and Safe Transitions

| Q | Stem | Keyed answer | Explanation | Objective |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Why can some glucose meters give falsely high readings in patients on icodextrin? | Icodextrin is broken down to maltose, which some meters read as glucose | Meters that cannot tell maltose from glucose over-read. |  |
| 2 | What is the main danger of that interference? | Missed hypoglycemia, or insulin given for a falsely high reading | A falsely normal reading can hide true hypoglycemia, and insulin can make it worse. |  |
| 3 | Why is low potassium more common in PD than in hemodialysis? | Continuous potassium removal and often poor intake | PD removes potassium every hour, and many patients eat little or still follow hemodialysis-era restrictions. |  |
| 4 | Old and new prescriptions conflict at discharge. What should the nurse do? | Pause the disputed instruction, get clarification from the treating team, then document and communicate the verified order | Never send a patient home with two versions of the order. |  |
| 5 | What makes culture follow-up closed-loop? | A named reviewer and timeframe, confirmation of review, and communication of the plan | A result nobody owns is a result nobody acts on. |  |
| 6 | The 2026 ISRNM and ISPD nutrition recommendations are notable because they: | Are the first international PD-specific nutrition practice recommendations, using GRADE and practice points | They mix graded recommendations with expert-consensus practice points. |  |
| 7 | Which item must be in a ward handoff for a PD patient? | The current PD order, delivery, next treatment and who will do it | The receiving team may never have handled PD. Tell them who does the next exchange. |  |
| 8 | Why is protein-energy wasting a concern in PD? | Daily effluent protein loss, inflammation and appetite suppression from glucose and abdominal fullness | Several mechanisms push in the same direction. |  |
| 9 | Mrs. Nenita avoids all fruit because of hemodialysis-era advice and has low potassium. Best action? | Report the result and her intake to the team and dietitian for individual advice | Potassium decisions belong to the team and dietitian. Do not leave the old rule standing, and do not replace it with your own. |  |
| 10 | What does the 2026 ISPD position statement recommend about “technique failure”? | Replace it with standardized definitions of each cause of loss from PD, and key performance indicators | Specific causes point to specific fixes. A vague label hides them. |  |
| 11 | After a long admission for peritonitis, what should discharge planning include? | Retraining and technique review, pending results with a reviewer, supplies and a follow-up date | Peritonitis and long admissions are both retraining triggers. |  |
| 12 | Mr. Lando and Mr. Toto both leave PD for hemodialysis. Which made Mr. Lando’s exit planned? | The falling trend was raised early, so a fistula was created while he was still on PD | Raising the predictive trend early allowed access planning and an outpatient start. |  |
| 13 | Why ask about constipation at routine visits? | It affects catheter drainage and is linked to enteric peritonitis risk | Report it; bowel management is a team decision. |  |
| 14 | Glucose absorbed from PD solution: | Adds calories that may help underweight patients and harm those with obesity, diabetes or high blood lipids | The same calories mean different things for Lola Ising and for Mr. Ben. |  |
| 15 | Mr. Rey is moving to another province. What should you send? | His order, records, recent labs and a named contact, and confirm device compatibility or training | A different cycler model needs training before he relies on it. |  |

## Module 11: Pediatric, Acute and Assisted PD

| Q | Stem | Keyed answer | Explanation | Objective |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | What must an assistance plan specify? | Task allocation, trained personnel, coverage, escalation, backup and supervision | Each element closes a gap where therapy could be missed or done unsafely. |  |
| 2 | Which team verifies pediatric prescriptions and infection protocols? | The pediatric nephrology and PD team, using age-appropriate standards | Adult defaults can harm a child. The pediatric team decides. |  |
| 3 | According to the 2020 ISPD acute PD guideline for adults, PD is: | A suitable modality for AKI in all settings (1B) | The guideline supports PD for AKI everywhere, with minimum-standard options for limited resources. |  |
| 4 | Which acute PD recommendation carries GRADE 1A? | A closed delivery system with a Y connection | The closed Y system is 1A (optimal) for adults and children. |  |
| 5 | Bicarbonate-containing solutions are recommended in acute PD particularly for: | Critically ill patients with significant liver dysfunction and marked lactate elevation (1B) | A damaged liver handles lactate poorly, so bicarbonate solutions are preferred. |  |
| 6 | Why is automated PD not suitable for newborns in the 2020 pediatric guideline? | Fill volumes are too small for current machines | The recommendation is graded 1D; it reflects machine limits, not unsuitability of PD itself. |  |
| 7 | How should the pediatric automated PD benefit be discussed with a family? | Verify current status with PhilHealth first, because the reviewed extension ended 31 July 2026 | Coverage after that date was not verified. Never promise a benefit you have not confirmed. |  |
| 8 | What does the 2024 ISPD position paper say about offering PD? | PD should be offered to every eligible person considering maintenance dialysis | Assistance is the tool for widening that access. |  |
| 9 | Observational studies comparing assisted and self-care PD in older patients found: | Broadly similar adjusted survival and peritonitis-free survival | They support feasibility but cannot prove equivalence. |  |
| 10 | Bea, 7, visiting from Manila, has a hazy bag and a stomachache. What is the adult PD nurse’s role? | Screen for instability, keep the bag, and call her pediatric PD team at once | Recognize urgently, hand over quickly. Pediatric protocols govern sampling and treatment. |  |
| 11 | Why is acute PD monitoring more intense than chronic PD? | Fluid, potassium, glucose, acid–base balance and blood pressure can change within hours | Unstable patients need hourly attention, not monthly review. |  |
| 12 | A stroke patient can explain PD but cannot manage the connections. First step? | With consent, assess his remaining abilities and the available caregivers, and coordinate evaluation for assisted PD | He keeps the tasks he can do safely; the rest are reassigned and verified. |  |
| 13 | Lola Felicidad skipped therapy every Sunday because her caregiver was off. What was missing from the plan? | Coverage for every day, with a named backup | Read the plan against the calendar. Every absence needs a name. |  |
| 14 | Catheter insertion by trained nephrologists in acute PD is: | Safe, with results equivalent to surgical insertion in patients without contraindications (1B) | This is one reason acute PD can be started where surgical teams are scarce. |  |
| 15 | Which statement fits this module’s level? | It provides orientation; independent practice needs additional training and authorization | Recognize, keep safe, hand over. Independent practice needs more. |  |

## Module 12: Philippine Practice, Records and Quality

| Q | Stem | Keyed answer | Explanation | Objective |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | What is the peritonitis rate for 4 episodes over 8 patient-years? | 0.50 episodes per patient-year | 4 divided by 8 is 0.50, above the 0.40 target. |  |
| 2 | At a rate of 0.50 episodes per patient-year, how many months between episodes? | 24 | 12 divided by 0.50 is 24 months. |  |
| 3 | 6 episodes over 20 patient-years gives: | 0.30 episodes per patient-year | 6 divided by 20 is 0.30, below the target. |  |
| 4 | 3 of 14 episodes are culture-negative. Is the unit within the ISPD 2022 target? | No, 21% exceeds the <15% target | 21% is above target. Review sampling, transport and laboratory methods. |  |
| 5 | What evidence should the head verify when hiring under a PD Z contract? | Current PRC authority, training evidence accepted for that facility, validation, staffing and contract evidence under current requirements | The contract and self-assessment tool define the evidence; check the current versions. |  |
| 6 | Who decides PhilHealth eligibility and payment? | Authorized benefits personnel under the contract and current annexes | You help assemble documents; you do not decide eligibility or payment. |  |
| 7 | Which body licenses health facilities? | DOH through HFSRB | PRC licenses people; DOH through HFSRB licenses facilities; PhilHealth contracts and pays. |  |
| 8 | What nurse-to-patient ratio does the PhilHealth PD First self-assessment tool state? | 1:20 | Do not import the hemodialysis ratio into PD. |  |
| 9 | How should relapsing peritonitis be counted in the rate? | As one episode, per ISPD counting conventions | Counting a relapse twice would inflate the rate. |  |
| 10 | What does the 2026 ISPD position statement recommend instead of “technique failure”? | Standardized definitions for each cause of loss from PD, with key performance indicators | Specific causes reveal specific fixes. |  |
| 11 | Why can a small unit’s peritonitis rate swing widely from year to year? | Small numbers; one or two episodes change the rate greatly | Look at 12-month trends and each episode’s cause. |  |
| 12 | Which is a balancing measure for an initiative to sample before antibiotics? | Time to first antibiotic dose | It checks that better sampling does not delay treatment. |  |
| 13 | 24 of 32 patients were peritonitis-free last year. Against the ISPD 2022 target: | 75%, below the >80% target | 24 divided by 32 is 75%. |  |
| 14 | Four patients were on PD for 365, 184, 90 and 365 days this year. About how many patient-years at risk is that? | About 2.75 | 1,004 days divided by 365.25 is about 2.75. Counting heads instead of time overstates the denominator. |  |
| 15 | Before counseling a patient on the PD Z-benefit amount, you should: | Confirm the current amount and inclusions with the PhilHealth Regional Office | Amounts and inclusions change. Refer to the benefits officer and the current issuance. |  |

# PhilHealth Liaison, Claims Officer and Clerk

## Module 1: Roles, Authority, and Policy

| Q | Stem | Keyed answer | Explanation | Objective |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Who may change the diagnosis recorded for a dialysis session? | The authorized treating clinician | An administrative edit would change the clinical evidence. |  |
| 2 | An SOP conflicts with a later PhilHealth circular. What goes in the policy register? | The workflow affected, the later source and its effective date, the owner, and the decision | The register records which source governs and who decided. |  |
| 3 | Which date usually decides which version of a rule governs a claim? | The service date | Read it against the effective date and transition clauses. |  |
| 4 | Which source governs when a vendor help page conflicts with a PhilHealth advisory? | The advisory | A vendor page explains steps; it cannot override an issuance. |  |
| 5 | What is the risk of a hold note without a due date? | No one owns the clock, so a filing period can lapse unnoticed | Holds need an owner and a date. |  |
| 6 | Who releases a refund to a patient? | Finance, through the approved process | Claims staff document the variance and route it. |  |
| 7 | What is the current hemodialysis package per qualifying session under Circular 2024-0023? | 6,350 pesos | 990,600 pesos is the annual maximum for 156 sessions. |  |
| 8 | How is the 6,350-peso package split? | 6,000 facility and 350 professional | 3,650 and 350 was the July 2024 split of the 4,000-peso rate. |  |
| 9 | A supervisor asks you to relabel an acute kidney injury discharge as stage 5 to fit a batch. What do you do? | Hold the claim, preserve the source, route the diagnosis question, and escalate the request | A request to misrepresent the record is escalated, not argued. |  |
| 10 | Where do you get written resolution when instructions stay unclear? | The PhilHealth Regional Office | Keep the written reply in the policy register. |  |
| 11 | Which phrase is the best way to ask a clinician about a record? | “Please confirm the diagnosis documented for the session on September 7.” | Neutral wording asks for the source to be checked. |  |
| 12 | An old binder shows 4,000 pesos per session. What do you do with it? | Mark the page superseded with the newer issuance and date | Old claims may still need the older rule. |  |
| 13 | What does a circular do that an advisory usually does not? | Sets rules | Advisories usually explain, remind, or set dates under existing rules. |  |
| 14 | Who owns code selection? | The coder or designated reviewer | Claims staff flag a mismatch; coders decide. |  |
| 15 | Why must every role have a named backup? | So a decision never waits on an absent person while a clock runs | Clocks keep running when people are on leave. |  |

## Module 2: Intake, Eligibility, and the Dialysis Database

| Q | Stem | Keyed answer | Explanation | Objective |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Does confirmed eligibility establish PDD enrollment? | No; PDD registration needs its own verification | Two questions, two records. |  |
| 2 | A patient needs urgent assessment while PDD is pending. What do you do? | Notify the clinical team immediately and continue verification in parallel | Treatment never waits for paperwork. |  |
| 3 | Can the facility charge a patient for PDD registration? | No | The benefit rules do not allow a registration charge. |  |
| 4 | A relative reports “about 150 sessions so far.” How do you record it? | As pending and unverified until confirmed through an authorized channel | Verbal counts are starting points. |  |
| 5 | Which sign at the desk is a reason to call the nurse now? | Breathlessness after a missed session | Fluid overload can be dangerous within hours. |  |
| 6 | Why can weakness or palpitations in a dialysis patient be urgent? | They can be signs of high potassium, which can stop the heart | Potassium builds up between sessions. |  |
| 7 | In a large US cohort, when were deaths more frequent among thrice-weekly hemodialysis patients? | On the day after the two-day gap | Foley and colleagues, 2011. |  |
| 8 | What kind of evidence links missed sessions to hospital stays and deaths? | Observational cohort studies | So the wording is “associated with.” |  |
| 9 | The PDD search returns a record with a slightly different surname. What do you do? | Hold, validate identity with approved evidence, and use the authorized correction channel | A close match is not a match. |  |
| 10 | Under immediate eligibility, what should staff avoid doing automatically? | Demanding proof of a minimum contribution history | Route issues through the authorized process. |  |
| 11 | Do sessions used at another unit count toward the patient’s annual allocation? | Yes, they count toward the same calendar-year allocation | Ask about other units at every transfer. |  |
| 12 | What should a message to the PRO about utilization contain? | Minimum identifiers, the dates, one clear question, and an authorized channel | Minimum necessary data, one question. |  |
| 13 | Which record answers “Is this person covered on this date?” | Eligibility result | PDD answers a different question. |  |
| 14 | What must the hemodialysis benefit have, besides eligibility? | Documented chronic kidney disease stage 5, PDD registration, a participating facility, and sessions remaining | All four apply. |  |
| 15 | A patient says his last session was four days ago. What is your first step? | Tell the nurse now, with exactly what he said | A long gap raises potassium and fluid risk. |  |

## Module 3: The Hemodialysis Benefit, Patient Charges, and the Agreement Form

| Q | Stem | Keyed answer | Explanation | Objective |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | What amount of the package goes to the professional? | 350 pesos per qualifying session | 6,000 pesos goes to the facility. |  |
| 2 | Can a signed premium agreement turn a covered kit into a payable extra? | No; the covered-item copay stays zero | Correct the bill. |  |
| 3 | What is the maximum annual hemodialysis package value per patient? | 990,600 pesos | 156 × 6,350 pesos. |  |
| 4 | A patient asks whether unused sessions roll over to next year. What is the answer? | No; they expire at year-end | The allocation is per calendar year. |  |
| 5 | Erythropoietin is billed separately, and nursing documented it as indicated. What do you do? | Correct the bill; the covered item’s copay is zero | Covered and indicated means no copay. |  |
| 6 | The patient chose HDF and signed a premium agreement. Does the hemodialysis benefit still apply? | Yes; show the benefit and charge only the documented allowed difference | The circular preserves the HD benefit. |  |
| 7 | Why do dialysis patients need erythropoietin? | Failing kidneys stop making the hormone that drives red-cell production | That is why anemia is common in kidney failure. |  |
| 8 | Why is an anticoagulant used during hemodialysis? | Blood clots when it touches the plastic circuit | Without it the filter blocks. |  |
| 9 | Who decides whether an item was clinically indicated? | The treating team | Billing asks; it does not decide. |  |
| 10 | Does the lifting of the 45-day benefit limit change the hemodialysis limit? | No; HD keeps its 156-session limit | PhilHealth Circular 2025-0007 excludes hemodialysis. |  |
| 11 | How often is the agreement form completed? | For each treatment session | A grouped claim still needs one per session. |  |
| 12 | Which is the better teach-back question? | “Can you tell me which item is included and which one we are still checking?” | Teach-back asks the family to say it back. |  |
| 13 | Why is 156 the session number? | Three sessions a week for 52 weeks | The standard schedule for adequate hemodialysis. |  |
| 14 | What did a 2022 Thai national study find about hemodialysis patients under universal coverage? | Half still faced catastrophic health spending | Coverage gaps still hurt families. |  |
| 15 | A family is told a charge will be refunded before anyone checked the record. What went wrong? | A promise was made before verification; promise a date to check instead | Give a date, not a guarantee. |  |

## Module 4: Session Evidence, Utilization, and the Yearly Count

| Q | Stem | Keyed answer | Explanation | Objective |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Does an emergency HD session in a stage 5 patient consume a session? | Yes; count it and check the paired codes in the circular | 90935 with N18.5, and it counts toward the 156. |  |
| 2 | What do you do with a schedule entry that has no attested record? | Hold claim inclusion pending verification that care was rendered and documented | A schedule is a promise, not a receipt. |  |
| 3 | A 40-minute machine run has no nursing note. What is your step? | Hold and ask nursing what was rendered | Claimability follows the record. |  |
| 4 | A patient is at #156 on November 15. What should the liaison do now? | Plan early: funding handoff, social service, and a clear explanation, with no interruption of treatment | Treatment continues. |  |
| 5 | Two rows show the same patient, same date, at different branches. Next step? | Investigate duplicate versus distinct service from source records before releasing either | Source records decide. |  |
| 6 | Which code pair does the circular give for documented emergency HD in stage 5 disease? | 90935 and N18.5 | RVS 90935 is the procedure; N18.5 is the diagnosis. |  |
| 7 | Does a cancelled session consume utilization? | No | Only rendered sessions count. |  |
| 8 | Who files the claim for an emergency session delivered at a hospital? | The hospital that rendered it | Your unit records it as consumed. |  |
| 9 | A session on January 2 is which number for the year? | #1 of the new year | The allocation resets each calendar year. |  |
| 10 | Dialysis for acute kidney injury goes through: | The hospital’s own claim and coding review | AKI is a different pathway. |  |
| 11 | What is associated with worse outcomes in a large US dialysis network? | Missed and shortened treatments | Obialo and colleagues, 2012; observational. |  |
| 12 | Six sessions remain, Monday-Wednesday-Friday from Monday, November 2. When is the last covered session? | November 13 | November 2, 4, 6, 9, 11, 13. |  |
| 13 | Which document can locate a record but not prove treatment? | A booking or transport log | Use it to find the record, not to replace it. |  |
| 14 | Why are patients who needed extra sessions after a hospital stay at risk? | Extra sessions come out of the same 156 and they run short before December | Project early. |  |
| 15 | What is the disposition of a completed session beyond the allocation? | Over cap with funding follow-up; the patient is still dialyzed | Coverage never decides treatment. |  |

## Module 5: The Claim Packet, Codes, and Diagnosis-Related Groups

| Q | Stem | Keyed answer | Explanation | Objective |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Does grouping two sessions remove the second agreement form? | No; each treatment session has its own agreement form | The circular requires one per session. |  |
| 2 | Can a PDF serve as the eSOA under eClaims 3.0? | No; submit XML from the approved system. A PDF may support local review | PhilHealth returns claims with a PDF statement. |  |
| 3 | Who corrects a CF4 date conflict? | The clinical author or coder, from the source record | Claims staff route it. |  |
| 4 | An agreement form for session 2 is unsigned. Can someone sign it today with last week’s date? | No; complete it with today’s date through the authorized owner and SOP | No backdating. |  |
| 5 | Is 90935 a diagnosis code? | No; it is an RVS procedure code. The diagnosis on a stage 5 HD claim is N18.5 | Two code books, two fields. |  |
| 6 | Where is end-stage kidney disease on dialysis coded in PhilHealth’s code set? | N18.5 | PhilHealth deleted N18.0; N18.6 is a US code. |  |
| 7 | A clerk copies N18.6 from a free website. What do you do? | Hold the draft, route the code to the coder, and log the error | Take codes only from approved sources. |  |
| 8 | Under case rates, do two patients with the same code but different severity get different payments? | No; the rate is fixed per case-rate code | That is the main limit of case rates. |  |
| 9 | Under DRG, which part of the coding can move a case into a heavier group? | Complications and other diagnoses recorded as secondary diagnoses | The full record decides the group. |  |
| 10 | During shadow billing, what decides what the hospital is paid? | Case rates; DRG grouping runs in parallel for testing | Grouping is tested, not yet paid. |  |
| 11 | Does the August 1, 2026 CF5 rule change how a freestanding center files outpatient HD? | No; CF5 applies to inpatient discharges | The outpatient package is unchanged. |  |
| 12 | What does the eSOA prove that the package rate does not? | The itemized financial record of that claim | Rate and statement measure different things. |  |
| 13 | Name one field to compare across CF2, CF4, the agreement forms, and the eSOA. | Service dates | Also identity and session count. |  |
| 14 | A hospital asks your center to add a secondary diagnosis to its inpatient claim. What do you do? | Decline; each facility’s coder codes its own claim from its own documentation | Share only verified facts, through the liaison. |  |
| 15 | Which program assigns a hospital case to a DRG? | The grouper | PhilHealth adopted the Thai grouping algorithm. |  |

## Module 6: eClaims 3.0, Filing Deadlines, and Acknowledgments

| Q | Stem | Keyed answer | Explanation | Objective |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Is a successfully generated local batch file evidence of filing? | No; check the official receiving workflow for an acknowledgment or claim reference | Only the official receipt proves receipt. |  |
| 2 | Who can grant or confirm a deadline exception? | PhilHealth, under the applicable issuance; a vendor ticket is only evidence | Follow the formal authority. |  |
| 3 | From which date was only eClaims 3.0 operational, with lower versions decommissioned? | July 1, 2026 | PhilHealth Advisory 2026-0027. |  |
| 4 | Trigger November 15, 60 calendar days counted from the next day. What is the deadline? | January 14 | November 16 is day 1. |  |
| 5 | Your region is named in a typhoon advisory granting 120 days. What do you record? | The advisory number, region, covered dates, and the recomputed deadline approved by the claims lead | Extensions rest on a dated advisory. |  |
| 6 | Since July 15, 2026, where do information technology requests to PhilHealth go? | The PhilHealth Ticketing System | PhilHealth Advisory 2026-0037. |  |
| 7 | Is a vendor “success” message proof of filing? | No; only the official acknowledgment or claim reference | Capture the official receipt. |  |
| 8 | The screen times out after you send. What is your first step? | Save evidence of the attempt, then check the portal and batch history | Check state before any retry. |  |
| 9 | What happened to the late-filing flexibility under Circular 2025-0006? | It ran from March 21 to September 21, 2025 and is closed | History, not a remedy. |  |
| 10 | What must the statement of account be under eClaims 3.0? | XML | PDF statements are returned. |  |
| 11 | Why set an internal target before the deadline? | To leave time to fix problems before the legal clock ends | Problems take time to fix. |  |
| 12 | Do calendar-day filing periods skip weekends and holidays? | No, unless an issuance says otherwise | Count every day. |  |
| 13 | Status shows “still unsure” after a timeout. What do you do? | Hold, escalate, and do not resend | Uncertainty is not permission to resend. |  |
| 14 | What does a transmission acknowledgment prove? | Receipt at that stage | Track adjudication separately. |  |
| 15 | What should you use to log in to eClaims? | Your own individual authorized account | Accounts make the audit trail. |  |

## Module 7: Returned Claims, Denials, and Escalation

| Q | Stem | Keyed answer | Explanation | Objective |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Do you reuse the ordinary filing clock for every denial? | No; verify the remedy-specific period against the notice and issuance | Each remedy has its own clock. |  |
| 2 | What should a useful PRO request include? | Authorized claim reference, dates, notice reason, facts, one precise question, and a secure channel | Short and specific. |  |
| 3 | A return arrived October 10 with a verified October 22 deadline. What is a sensible internal target? | Several working days earlier, for example October 17 | Leave time for review. |  |
| 4 | Is a denial for “duplicate” automatically correct? | No; compare claim references and treatment evidence first | Two distinct sessions can be mistaken for one. |  |
| 5 | Who signs a motion for reconsideration or an appeal? | The authorized signatory named in the facility adoption record | Authority is assigned in writing. |  |
| 6 | What must never be overwritten during a correction? | The original record and notice | Add corrections with an audit trail. |  |
| 7 | A returned claim is: | A deficiency to fix and refile through the linked process | A denial is the decision. |  |
| 8 | Twelve claims are returned for the same missing signature. What is this? | A process defect to fix at the step that let them out | Returns are a quality signal. |  |
| 9 | Which entry do you correct when a claim shows “received” and “draft”? | Match the notice’s claim reference to the received record and correct through the linked workflow | Never turn a draft into a new claim. |  |
| 10 | A claim is paid, but less than expected. Who owns the first look? | Claims and finance together | Document the variance and review the remedy. |  |
| 11 | Where do you confirm the remedy and deadline when the notice is unclear? | Written guidance from the PRO and the applicable issuance | Keep the written reply. |  |
| 12 | What is the first entry in a case chronology? | The service date or dates | Service comes first. |  |
| 13 | The same session was really sent twice after a timeout. What next? | Refer for authorized disposition; do not refile | This is why Module 6 says check before resending. |  |
| 14 | Which identifiers go in a message to the PRO? | Only those the question needs | Minimum necessary data. |  |
| 15 | Why keep one chronology per case? | So anyone can understand the case quickly and the trail survives staff changes | It protects the claim and the facility. |  |

## Module 8: Payments, Reconciliation, and Professional Fees

| Q | Stem | Keyed answer | Explanation | Objective |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Can a deposit alone establish that all claims in a batch were paid? | No; match it to detailed remittance and ledger allocations | Deposits hide partial payments. |  |
| 2 | When does the professional-fee release clock start under Circular 2024-0023? | At receipt of PhilHealth reimbursement | Release within one month of receipt. |  |
| 3 | Five allowed sessions. What are the gross, facility, and professional amounts? | 31,750, 30,000, and 1,750 pesos | 6,350, 6,000, and 350 times five. |  |
| 4 | Reimbursement was received March 3. When must professional fees be released? | Within one month, by April 3 (confirm local counting convention) | One month from receipt. |  |
| 5 | 120 claims in the cohort, 9 initially returned. What is the return rate? | 7.5% | 9 ÷ 120. |  |
| 6 | Which cases are excluded from performance rates? | Unsubmitted cases, tracked separately | Rates describe adjudicated claims. |  |
| 7 | Two allowed sessions and one 6,350-peso posting found. How is the rest classified? | Unexplained until confirmed, with an owner | Never assume. |  |
| 8 | Why must aging start from a labeled point? | So months can be compared honestly | Receipt, transmission, or service date, chosen and stated. |  |
| 9 | Who records professional-fee disbursement evidence? | Finance | Claims staff track; finance disburses. |  |
| 10 | Four sessions claimed, three allowed. What is the difference from claimed? | 6,350 pesos, with a denial workflow open | 25,400 minus 19,050. |  |
| 11 | What is first-pass acceptance? | Claims paid without an initial return, over the adjudicated cohort | Fixed definitions make months comparable. |  |
| 12 | Returned 9, denied 3, and first-pass paid 115 in a cohort of 120. What is wrong? | The parts add up to more than the cohort; check the data | Calculator 4 refuses this. |  |
| 13 | Where do patient refunds go? | Through the approved refund or correction workflow | Keep the accounts separate. |  |
| 14 | Which three records does a three-way match compare? | Claim register, remittance detail, finance ledger | Claim, payment advice, and posting. |  |
| 15 | Why does reconciliation matter to patients? | Payments fund the unit that keeps them on dialysis, and unmatched deposits hide problems | Finance sustains care. |  |

## Module 9: Privacy, Accreditation, and Audit Readiness

| Q | Stem | Keyed answer | Explanation | Objective |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Who determines whether mandatory breach notification applies? | The personal information controller, through its incident process with the DPO | Claims staff report and record. |  |
| 2 | What is the risk of borrowing another branch’s accreditation number? | It misrepresents the rendering facility and undermines accreditation and claim evidence | Each site, each date. |  |
| 3 | A training screenshot contains a real PIN. What do you do? | Replace it with synthetic data and report it if it was already shared | Training uses fictional data only. |  |
| 4 | Accreditation renewal is pending on a service date. What about release? | Hold until participation for that date is confirmed | Validity on the service date matters. |  |
| 5 | Why is minimum necessary safer in emails to PhilHealth too? | Every extra identifier widens the harm if the email is misdirected | Misdirection happens to everyone. |  |
| 6 | What is the first step after sending data to the wrong recipient? | Stop sending more | Then contain, preserve, record, and report. |  |
| 7 | Within what period are the NPC and affected people generally notified when notification is required? | 72 hours | Under the applicable rule. |  |
| 8 | Which law protects patients’ personal data in the Philippines? | RA 10173, the Data Privacy Act | The Data Privacy Act of 2012. |  |
| 9 | Is a personal chat account an approved channel for patient data? | No | Use the facility’s approved channels. |  |
| 10 | Why are dialysis claims data especially sensitive? | They include diagnoses, hepatitis and HIV results, relationships, and finances | Sensitive personal information. |  |
| 11 | When you correct a record, what do you keep? | The original entry and the audit history of the change | Corrections never erase. |  |
| 12 | Which document proves a claim was filed once? | The acknowledgment or claim reference with time stamp | An auditor will ask. |  |
| 13 | Whose quality standards does PhilHealth use as its reference for maintenance hemodialysis? | Its own Circular 2024-0029 | Quality standards on maintenance hemodialysis. |  |
| 14 | When should accreditation renewal reminders be set? | Well before expiry, for example at 90, 60, and 30 days | A facility reminder practice. |  |
| 15 | Who leads the facility’s privacy incident response? | The Data Protection Officer | Report to the DPO at once. |  |

## Module 10: Renal Pathways and the Patient Handoff

| Q | Stem | Keyed answer | Explanation | Objective |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Can the maintenance stage 5 allowance be assumed for temporary dialysis for acute kidney injury? | No; route it to hospital claims review under the applicable pathway | The diagnosis decides the route. |  |
| 2 | What belongs in a transfer handoff? | Verified status, last treatment, utilization, open questions, authorized receiver, and next action | Close the loop. |  |
| 3 | Does peritoneal dialysis under the Z benefit require PDD registration? | Yes | Circular 2024-0036. |  |
| 4 | A hemodialysis-accredited center wants to bill a transplant work-up under the Z benefit. Allowed? | Only if it is a contracted Z provider for that package | Contracting is separate from HD accreditation. |  |
| 5 | A patient on AKI dialysis is discharged still needing dialysis. Who decides the pathway? | The nephrologist documents the diagnosis; hospital coding routes the claim | Some patients progress to chronic disease. |  |
| 6 | Why do AKI and stage 5 chronic kidney disease (CKD) lead to different benefits? | AKI is sudden and may recover; stage 5 is a lasting loss | Crutch versus replacement. |  |
| 7 | A patient says PhilHealth pays for a transplant at any hospital. What is accurate? | The Z benefit is delivered through contracted centers, which assess eligibility | Verify with the receiving service. |  |
| 8 | Who quotes the amount of a Z benefit to the patient? | The contracted service, from the current circular | Amounts change by circular. |  |
| 9 | A patient asks to switch to peritoneal dialysis next month. First step? | Record the request, contact the clinical team and a contracted PD coordinator, and agree a callback | Close the loop. |  |
| 10 | What does “closing the loop” on a referral need? | An owner, an acknowledgment of receipt, and a callback time | A referral sent is not a referral received. |  |
| 11 | An emergency HD session during a confinement: what does your unit record? | The session as consumed, with the hospital’s claim reference, and no billing for that date | The hospital files it. |  |
| 12 | A patient raises an urgent symptom during a coverage conversation. What do you do? | Hand over directly to nursing or the treating team | Clinical first, always. |  |
| 13 | Which phrase separates verified from pending? | “Your eligibility is confirmed; the center will tell us whether you qualify for the transplant benefit.” | Never promise what is not yet determined. |  |
| 14 | Where does possible financial help go? | The facility’s social service pathway | Route it; do not improvise. |  |
| 15 | Which document decides which benefit a session belongs to? | The clinician-documented diagnosis and modality | Never route by convenience. |  |

## Module 11: Capstone Practicum and Job Aids

| Q | Stem | Keyed answer | Explanation | Objective |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | PRO-confirmed utilization is 153. What number is the next completed session? | #154 | Count from the confirmed total. |  |
| 2 | A scheduled session is cancelled. What does it consume? | Nothing | Only rendered sessions count. |  |
| 3 | Who files a confirmed emergency HD session delivered during a hospital confinement? | The hospital that rendered it | Keep the hospital’s claim reference. |  |
| 4 | An attested session has an unsigned agreement form. What is its disposition? | Counts toward utilization; claim held pending the agreement form | Route to the source owner without backdating. |  |
| 5 | The register says “draft,” but official acknowledgment SIM-A01 exists. What do you do? | Update the register, do not resend, and refer any correction of the received claim to the authorized officer | A resend would be a duplicate. |  |
| 6 | Which of these is a critical error in the capstone? | Billing a covered service to the patient after identifying it as covered | Critical errors are judged before the score. |  |
| 7 | A candidate scores 95 but releases a duplicate claim. Result? | Does not pass | The critical-error gate overrides points. |  |
| 8 | What is the pass standard? | 80 of 100 or more with zero critical errors | Both conditions. |  |
| 9 | SIM-B01 allows two sessions. What is the split of 12,700 pesos? | 12,000 facility and 700 professional | 6,000 and 350 per session. |  |
| 10 | A return has a verified deadline of October 22. What earns full credit? | Routing it by October 22 and keeping the linked receipt | Returns use the linked process. |  |
| 11 | A colleague suggests a personal chat account for a real patient file in training. Response? | Use only fictional data, name an approved channel for real work, and escalate any actual disclosure | Unauthorized disclosure is a critical error. |  |
| 12 | Which three questions frame the debrief? | Which record shows the session occurred, which proves filing, and who owns the next action | Evidence, filing, ownership. |  |
| 13 | When is Form B used? | To reassess a candidate with different facts after a debrief | Same structure, new facts. |  |
| 14 | What must a claims lead complete before staff use the course for live work? | The facility adoption record, with the facility head | Local settings need written approval. |  |
| 15 | How many supervised workplace observations does sign-off require? | Three: intake, submission simulation, and an exception handoff | Recorded with date, observer, evidence, and outcome. |  |

# Dialysis Unit Operations Manager

## Module 1: Who Runs the Dialysis Unit

| Q | Stem | Keyed answer | Explanation | Objective |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Who assigns nurses to patients for a shift? | The authorized nursing lead | Nursing assignment requires delegated nursing authority. The manager supports it with the roster but does not make it unless also appointed to a nursing role. |  |
| 2 | A physician phones you, the manager, with a change in a patient's dialysate potassium. What do you do? | Transfer the call to the nurse or POD who can receive and document the order | Orders go to a licensed clinician who can read back, document and execute them. A manager relaying orders adds an error-prone step. |  |
| 3 | Which power does a well-written policy usually give the operations manager? | Authority to place a safety hold, with release reserved for technical and clinical owners | Stopping is reversible and low-risk. Releasing requires expertise, so it stays with the designated technical and clinical authorities. |  |
| 4 | What closes an escalation? | A named person accepts it and records the next action and time | Closure needs receipt and ownership. Without them, the issue is still open, however many people know about it. |  |
| 5 | In the I-PASS study, what happened to preventable adverse events after a structured handoff program? | They fell by about 30% | Preventable adverse events fell from 4.7 to 3.3 per 100 admissions, and handoff duration did not change. |  |
| 6 | One person is both head nurse and unit manager. What protects the unit? | A written appointment covering both roles and stating which role applies to each decision | Dual roles are legitimate when written. The appointment defines scope and prevents disputes later. |  |
| 7 | Under DOH AO 2012-0001, which role leads QAPI, infection surveillance and water safety oversight? | The Medical Director | The order places technical and medical oversight, including QAPI, infection surveillance and water safety, with the Medical Director. |  |
| 8 | The owner tells you to seat all booked patients even though one nurse is absent. The best first step is to: | Ask the head nurse how many stations can be safely staffed now | Coverage decides capacity. The nursing lead determines what is safe, and you build the opening plan around her answer. |  |
| 9 | Which item belongs on a decision-rights map? | Owner, backup, escalation trigger and deadline for each decision | A decision map works only if it names who decides, who covers, when to escalate and by when. |  |
| 10 | RA 9173 §29 is relevant to a unit manager because it: | Sets minimum qualifications for supervisory nursing positions, if the role is one | HR must determine whether the specific position falls under §29 and document the decision. |  |
| 11 | A patient's relative asks you for a copy of the treatment record. You should: | Route the request through the facility's data privacy process | RA 10173 treats health data as sensitive personal information. Release follows the authorized process. |  |
| 12 | What makes an opening huddle closed-loop? | The receiver repeats the action, the owner and the check time | Read-back confirms the message was received as intended. |  |
| 13 | Which statement about governance evidence is most accurate? | Comparable regulators separate governing body, medical director and nurse manager roles, but outcome trials of these structures are lacking | These are policy designs, not trial results. We use them as informed models. |  |
| 14 | Who is responsible for releasing a machine back to service after repair? | The designated biomedical technician, according to the approved procedure | Technical release requires a trained person following a documented checklist. |  |
| 15 | The primary POD is unreachable 20 minutes before opening. What is the correct sequence? | Call the listed backup, record times, hold stations without coverage and notify the Medical Director | A coverage list exists for this moment. You document, hold what is not covered and escalate. |  |

## Module 2: Licensure, Accreditation, and Survey Readiness

| Q | Stem | Keyed answer | Explanation | Objective |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Which body issues the license to operate a hemodialysis facility? | DOH through the Center for Health Development | The LTO is a DOH permission. PhilHealth and PSN carry separate processes. |  |
| 2 | What does PhilHealth accreditation primarily allow? | Filing claims for covered benefit packages | Accreditation concerns payment. Operation requires the LTO. |  |
| 3 | The unit installs three new machines in an unapproved room and starts using them. The main problem is: | Operating beyond the approved scope of the LTO | Station count and location form part of the approved scope. Expansion needs prior approval. |  |
| 4 | What is the most useful single checklist for preparing a DOH inspection? | The current DOH assessment tool for your facility type | The assessment tool is the inspector's own form. |  |
| 5 | In a tracer, what does the surveyor follow? | One patient or one item through every step of care | Tracers expose handoff gaps between people and departments. |  |
| 6 | A water log lacks a signature from last month. What is the correct fix? | Add a dated late entry with explanation and open a CAPA | Late entries with the current date are honest. Back-dating is falsification. |  |
| 7 | Which part of a CAPA proves the system fix worked? | The effectiveness check with a target, owner and due date | Effectiveness checks show the problem did not recur. |  |
| 8 | When must PDD registration be completed for outpatient package claims? | Before the sessions are claimed, following PhilHealth rules | Registration establishes eligibility for the package. |  |
| 9 | In the CDC collaborative, what was associated with lower bloodstream infections? | A bundle that included routine audits with feedback | Scheduled audits and feedback were core elements of the bundle. |  |
| 10 | An evidence index row should include: | Requirement, source, evidence, location, owner and expiry | Each field answers a question an inspector will ask. |  |
| 11 | An amber row in the evidence index means: | Due within 60 days or incomplete | Amber flags work that must happen soon. Red means expired or missing. |  |
| 12 | Who reviews the Dialysis Clinical Head appointment as part of professional attestation? | PSN | PSN's attestation process reviews the appointment and related documents. |  |
| 13 | During a tracer, a nurse says, "I just know the dialyzer was tested." This is: | A finding, because the evidence is not documented | If it is not written, it cannot be verified. |  |
| 14 | What is the root cause in this finding: "Afternoon logs unsigned because the reviewer works only mornings"? | No reviewer was assigned to afternoon logs | Root causes describe the system gap, not individual blame. |  |
| 15 | Which statement about readiness is most accurate? | Readiness is a daily filing discipline, not a pre-inspection project | Units that keep evidence current handle unannounced visits calmly. |  |

## Module 3: Staffing, Rostering, and Competency

| Q | Stem | Keyed answer | Explanation | Objective |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | The DOH nurse-to-station figure of 1:4 is best understood as: | A ceiling that must be tightened for acuity, isolation and breaks | The figure sets the minimum cover. Real conditions often require more. |  |
| 2 | Fifteen stations are in use. How many POD positions does the 1:15 ceiling require at minimum? | One | ceiling(15 ÷ 15) = 1. A POD must be present during operating hours. |  |
| 3 | What does a relief factor account for? | Leave, holidays, training and sick time that reduce productive hours | Relief converts positions into the number of people needed to fill them reliably. |  |
| 4 | Non-productive time is 20%. What is the relief factor? | 1.25 | 1 ÷ (1 − 0.20) = 1.25. |  |
| 5 | In the 2024 US cohort study, the highest quartile of patient-to-technician ratio was associated with: | About 7% higher mortality and 5% higher hospitalization | incidence rate ratio (IRR) 1.07 for mortality and 1.05 for hospitalization, observational data. |  |
| 6 | Why can't the staffing study prove that adding staff improves survival? | It is observational, so other facility differences may explain the association | Association is not causation. Confounding cannot be fully excluded. |  |
| 7 | A new nurse's cannulation competency is marked "supervised only." On the roster you must: | Assign a named qualified supervisor on the same shift | Restrictions must be visible and supervised. |  |
| 8 | Who approves a reallocated nursing roster? | The nursing lead | Nursing assignment requires nursing authority. |  |
| 9 | Which patients need your strongest available staff after an absence? | Isolation patients and new starts | These groups carry the highest procedural and clinical risk. |  |
| 10 | A tired nurse offers to work a second shift. The best decision is usually to: | Decline and reduce stations or reschedule safely | Fatigue impairs performance in ways the person may not notice. |  |
| 11 | Under Labor Code Art. 83, health personnel in large cities or large facilities normally work: | 40 hours a week | HR must confirm which workers and facilities are covered. |  |
| 12 | A meta-analysis of 85 studies associated nurse burnout with: | More infections, falls and medication errors, and lower patient satisfaction | Burnout is consistently associated with worse safety and quality. |  |
| 13 | Fourteen non-isolation stations at 1:4 need how many nurse positions? | Four | ceiling(14 ÷ 4) = 4. |  |
| 14 | When a competency counts on the matrix, it must be: | Signed and dated by a qualified assessor | Observed and signed skills are the proof. |  |
| 15 | If the ratio cannot be met even after calling replacements, you should: | Reduce stations for that shift and reschedule within the treatment window | Capacity follows safe staffing, never the other way around. |  |

## Module 4: Capacity, Access, and Patient Flow

| Q | Stem | Keyed answer | Explanation | Objective |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Why is the day after the long interdialytic interval the riskiest? | Potassium, fluid and acid have accumulated for the longest time | The two-day gap allows the largest buildup of solutes and volume. |  |
| 2 | In DOPPS, skipping at least one session a month was associated with: | About 30% higher mortality risk | RR 1.30 in an observational cohort. |  |
| 3 | A unit has 12 stations, 3 shifts and 6 days. How many weekly station-sessions are available? | 216 | 12 × 3 × 6 = 216. |  |
| 4 | How many thrice-weekly patients can 216 station-sessions support? | 72 | 216 ÷ 3 = 72. |  |
| 5 | Who decides to shorten a patient's treatment time? | The attending nephrologist | Treatment time is part of the prescription. |  |
| 6 | What did research show about rescheduling a missed session to the next day? | It reduced but did not fully eliminate the excess risk | Next-day treatment helps, but the missed session still carries harm. |  |
| 7 | Utilization is 98%. What is the main operational concern? | No buffer for make-up sessions after an outage | Very high utilization leaves no slack for disruptions. |  |
| 8 | PhilHealth's 156-session annual cap is based on: | Three sessions a week for 52 weeks | The package was set to match thrice-weekly dialysis. |  |
| 9 | Which detail is essential before placing a referred patient on a station? | Serology status, to decide whether isolation is needed | Bloodborne virus status determines station and staff assignment. |  |
| 10 | In DOPPS, treatment times longer than 240 minutes were associated with: | Lower mortality | RR 0.81, observational data. |  |
| 11 | A holiday closure would create a five-day gap for some patients. The best response is to: | Move their sessions to adjacent days so no gap exceeds three days | Rescheduling keeps the interval clinically acceptable. |  |
| 12 | Which patients get first priority after an outage? | Those coming off the long gap, with high potassium, or catheter-dependent | Prioritize by physiologic risk. |  |
| 13 | Why should referral decisions be documented with reasons? | To make acceptance fair and defensible | Written criteria protect patients and the unit. |  |
| 14 | An isolation station used for only two patients represents: | Idle capacity that should be stated openly in planning | Isolation capacity is reserved by design and should not be counted as open. |  |
| 15 | Ultrafiltration rates above 10 mL/kg/h were associated in DOPPS with: | More intradialytic hypotension | Fast fluid removal outpaces vascular refilling. |  |

## Module 5: Infection Prevention and Surveillance

| Q | Stem | Keyed answer | Explanation | Objective |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Why do catheter patients have higher bloodstream infection rates than fistula patients? | The catheter hub is handled repeatedly and biofilm forms on the device | The CVC provides a direct, colonizable path into the bloodstream. |  |
| 2 | In the CDC collaborative, access-related BSIs fell by about: | 54% | Overall BSIs fell 32% and access-related BSIs fell 54%. |  |
| 3 | The CDC collaborative design limits certainty because it: | Was a before-and-after study without a control group | Secular trends may contribute, so the evidence supports rather than proves benefit. |  |
| 4 | Eighty patient-months and four BSIs give a rate of: | 5.0 per 100 patient-months | 4 ÷ 80 × 100 = 5.0. |  |
| 5 | A key failure in the 1994 HBV outbreaks was: | Screening results that were not routinely reviewed | Unread results meant infected patients were not isolated. |  |
| 6 | HBsAg-positive patients should dialyze: | In a separate area with dedicated machines, supplies and staff | HBV transmits easily through invisible blood contamination. |  |
| 7 | How long can HBV survive on environmental surfaces? | At least seven days | Environmental persistence is why surface disinfection matters. |  |
| 8 | A shared multidose heparin vial is risky because: | It can carry blood contamination between patients | Shared vials have been implicated in HBV outbreaks. |  |
| 9 | In the CDC approach, what makes a hand-hygiene audit effective? | Regular observation with feedback to staff | Feedback closes the loop and changes behavior. |  |
| 10 | 120 observed opportunities, 90 compliant. What is compliance? | 75% | 90 ÷ 120 × 100 = 75%. |  |
| 11 | A new positive HBsAg result should trigger: | Same-day isolation decision, Medical Director notification and contact review | Delay allows transmission. |  |
| 12 | What does KDIGO's 2018 HCV guideline emphasize for dialysis units? | Rigorous standard precautions and audits rather than routine isolation | Local policy, PSN guidelines and DOH rules determine your practice. |  |
| 13 | Why is a single month's BSI rate in a small unit hard to interpret? | One event can change the rate dramatically | Use run charts and trends rather than single values. |  |
| 14 | Which management action most directly reduces BSIs over time? | Reducing catheter prevalence through timely fistula referral | Catheters drive most dialysis BSIs. |  |
| 15 | A transient patient arrives for one session. Before treatment you need: | Current serology results | Serology determines station placement and isolation. |  |

## Module 6: Water and Dialysate Readiness

| Q | Stem | Keyed answer | Explanation | Objective |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Why must dialysis water be purer than drinking water? | Patients are exposed to over a hundred liters per session through a thin membrane | Volume and direct blood exposure make trace contaminants dangerous. |  |
| 2 | What do chlorine and chloramine cause if they reach a patient? | Hemolysis and methemoglobinemia | Oxidants damage red blood cells. |  |
| 3 | Why are carbon beds usually installed in series? | So breakthrough in the first bed is caught before water moves on | The second bed provides a safety margin. |  |
| 4 | The ISO maximum for viable microbes in dialysis water is: | Less than 100 CFU/mL | Ultrapure fluid has the 0.1 CFU/mL limit. |  |
| 5 | The ISO maximum endotoxin level for dialysis water is: | Less than 0.25 EU/mL | Older US limits allowed 2 EU/mL. |  |
| 6 | What does reaching an action level mean? | Investigate, disinfect and retest before the maximum is reached | Action levels are early warnings. |  |
| 7 | Which part of the system most commonly develops bacterial regrowth? | Storage and distribution loop | Low flow and dead legs promote biofilm. |  |
| 8 | A morning chlorine result is missing. You should: | Hold the opening until the test is run and released | Missing evidence means the water is not released. |  |
| 9 | Who releases water after a hold? | The authorized technical and clinical owners, by signature | Release requires technical interpretation and clinical accountability. |  |
| 10 | What caused the 1996 Caruaru tragedy? | Cyanobacterial toxins in dialysis water | Microcystins caused acute liver failure and dozens of deaths. |  |
| 11 | Which result needs action under ISO defaults: 62 CFU/mL in dialysis water? | Above the action level, below the maximum | Action level 50, maximum 100. |  |
| 12 | Which document sets the controlling numbers in a Philippine unit? | DOH AO 2013-0003 and the facility's approved water program | Local regulation and the approved program govern. |  |
| 13 | A water log shows identical values every day for a month in the same handwriting. This suggests: | Possible copied or fabricated entries needing investigation | Real measurements vary. Uniformity is a red flag. |  |
| 14 | Is ultrapure dialysate proven to improve survival in routine hemodialysis? | Not established; observational data link it to lower inflammation | Benefit on hard outcomes remains unproven. |  |
| 15 | Why include dialyzer reprocessing in the water sampling map? | Reprocessing uses treated water that must meet the same standard | Contaminated rinse water contaminates reused dialyzers. |  |

## Module 7: Emergency Readiness and Incident Command

| Q | Stem | Keyed answer | Explanation | Objective |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Why can venous needle dislodgement cause rapid blood loss? | The blood pump continues returning blood at high flow if the alarm fails to stop it | Pump flow rates of 300 mL/min or more continue until stopped. |  |
| 2 | During a clinical emergency, the operations manager's main role is to: | Communicate externally, protect the space and keep other patients monitored | Clinicians treat; the manager coordinates. |  |
| 3 | In the Hurricane Sandy study, early dialysis before the storm was associated with: | Lower 30-day mortality, about 28% lower odds | Observational data support early dialysis before predictable disasters. |  |
| 4 | What is a near miss? | An event that could have caused harm but did not | Near misses reveal hazards at no cost to patients. |  |
| 5 | In just culture, widening alarm limits to avoid nuisance alarms is usually: | At-risk behavior to be coached and designed out | It is a drifted shortcut, often driven by a system problem. |  |
| 6 | Who usually leads clinical resuscitation in the unit? | The physician on duty | The physician on duty (POD) provides medical leadership during the shift. |  |
| 7 | What should a root cause analysis ask? | What in the system allowed the event | System questions produce durable fixes. |  |
| 8 | A facility drill is most useful when it: | Includes actual disconnection and evacuation practice | Hands-on rehearsal reveals real gaps. |  |
| 9 | Disclosure of harm to a patient means: | Honest, prompt explanation through the designated clinician | Disclosure is a professional obligation. |  |
| 10 | Hyperkalemia is dangerous because it: | Slows cardiac conduction and can cause fatal arrhythmia | Potassium changes the resting membrane potential of cardiac cells. |  |
| 11 | How often should the emergency cart seal be checked? | Daily, with full content and expiry checks monthly or per policy | Expired drugs hide behind intact seals. |  |
| 12 | During evacuation, what is checked at the assembly point? | Everyone is accounted for against the shift list | Accountability prevents leaving someone behind. |  |
| 13 | After a power failure stops the blood pump, staff must: | Return blood manually or follow the emergency procedure as directed | Manual return limits blood loss and clotting. |  |
| 14 | Why should access sites remain visible during treatment? | So needle dislodgement and bleeding are seen immediately | Covered sites hide bleeding. |  |
| 15 | Treating every error as reckless behavior tends to: | Reduce incident reporting | Fear suppresses the information needed to fix systems. |  |

## Module 8: Equipment, Supplies, and Procurement

| Q | Stem | Keyed answer | Explanation | Objective |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | What does FEFO mean? | First expiry, first out | Use the earliest-expiring lot first. |  |
| 2 | Daily usage 30, lead time 7 days, safety stock 10 days. What is the reorder point? | 510 | 30 × 7 + 30 × 10 = 210 + 300 = 510. |  |
| 3 | Stock on hand 600, daily usage 40. Days on hand? | 15 | 600 ÷ 40 = 15. |  |
| 4 | Why record the lot number of dialyzers and bloodlines per patient? | So affected patients can be identified quickly in a recall | Traceability makes recalls fast and accurate. |  |
| 5 | A reprocessed dialyzer must: | Belong to one patient, be labeled and pass performance and germicide checks | Process control defines reuse safety. |  |
| 6 | What is the evidence position on dialyzer reuse? | Mixed observational data; safety depends on process control | Well-controlled reprocessing is the defensible standard. |  |
| 7 | A switch to a new dialyzer brand needs approval from: | The Medical Director, after clinical review | Membrane differences can affect delivered dose. |  |
| 8 | Scheduled machine hours 1,000; unavailable 50. Availability? | 95% | (1,000 − 50) ÷ 1,000 × 100 = 95%. |  |
| 9 | In a recall, what happens first after verifying the notice? | Physically quarantine and label the affected stock | Quarantine prevents further use. |  |
| 10 | The three-way match compares: | Purchase order, receiving report and invoice | Payment follows a verified match. |  |
| 11 | Why choose a larger safety stock for a single-importer product? | A single source carries higher disruption risk | Safety stock buffers supply uncertainty. |  |
| 12 | After a machine repair, before returning it to service: | A trained technician documents a release check | Documented release confirms safe function. |  |
| 13 | One machine has been repaired five times in six months. This most likely indicates: | A retirement or replacement decision | Track repairs per asset to support lifecycle decisions. |  |
| 14 | Who should not be the same person in procurement, where possible? | The requester, approver, receiver and payer | Segregation of duties prevents error and fraud. |  |
| 15 | Supplier lead time increases from 10 to 20 days. The reorder point: | Rises by usage × 10 days | Longer lead time requires ordering earlier. |  |

## Module 9: Quality Measurement and Improvement

| Q | Stem | Keyed answer | Explanation | Objective |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | What minimum delivered spKt/V per thrice-weekly session does KDOQI (2015) set? | 1.2 | The target is 1.4, the minimum delivered dose 1.2. |  |
| 2 | Why is Kt/V called a surrogate? | It is a laboratory-derived process measure, not a patient-experienced outcome | Surrogates must be paired with hard outcomes. |  |
| 3 | What did the HEMO trial show? | A dose higher than standard did not improve survival | More is not always better once adequacy is achieved. |  |
| 4 | Under run-chart rules, a shift is: | Six or more consecutive points on one side of the median | Shifts signal non-random change. |  |
| 5 | A trend on a run chart is: | Five or more consecutive increases or decreases | Standard conventions reduce false alarms. |  |
| 6 | 1,200 scheduled sessions and 30 missed. Rate per 100? | 2.5 | 30 ÷ 1,200 × 100 = 2.5. |  |
| 7 | What belongs in a data dictionary entry? | Numerator, denominator, exclusions, source, owner and frequency | Clear definitions make trends trustworthy. |  |
| 8 | Fistula use jumps 13 points in a month. The first step is to: | Check whether the definition or data extraction changed | Sudden jumps often reflect measurement changes. |  |
| 9 | Which aim statement is best? | Reduce missed sessions from 3.1 to below 2.0 per 100 by June 30 | Aims need a measure, target and deadline. |  |
| 10 | Who chairs the QAPI program under Department of Health (DOH) Administrative Order (AO) 2012-0001? | The Medical Director | QAPI is a Medical Director function. |  |
| 11 | In PDSA, the "Study" step involves: | Comparing results with the prediction | Learning comes from prediction versus result. |  |
| 12 | Why does KDIGO caution against routinely targeting high hemoglobin with ESAs? | Higher targets have been associated with cardiovascular and stroke harm | Trials showed harm at high targets. |  |
| 13 | The last six monthly values all sit below the median after a new reminder system. This suggests: | A real improvement linked to the change | A shift after a change supports its effect. |  |
| 14 | Why include hospitalization in a quality dashboard? | It is a patient-important outcome that surrogates may miss | Outcomes anchor process measures to what matters. |  |
| 15 | A common failure in PDSA projects is: | Testing changes too large and skipping the Study step | Small, documented cycles work better. |  |

## Module 10: PhilHealth Claims, Documentation, and Privacy

| Q | Stem | Keyed answer | Explanation | Objective |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | What is the current PhilHealth maintenance hemodialysis (HD) session rate under PC 2024-0023 (verify locally)? | ₱6,350 | The rate rose to ₱6,350 effective October 9, 2024. |  |
| 2 | How many sessions per calendar year does the package cover? | Up to 156 | 156 sessions match three a week for 52 weeks. |  |
| 3 | The maximum annual benefit at ₱6,350 × 156 is: | ₱990,600 | 6,350 × 156 = 990,600. |  |
| 4 | When should the attending and patient sign claim documents? | After the session is delivered | Pre-signing creates fraud risk and claim denials. |  |
| 5 | PDD registration for the patient is: | Free and required for the outpatient package | Registration establishes eligibility at no cost. |  |
| 6 | A patient has used 150 sessions by November 24. At three per week, roughly how many sessions are needed to December 31? | 16 | 5.3 weeks × 3 = 16 sessions. |  |
| 7 | In the example above, what is the shortfall? | 10 sessions | 16 needed − 6 remaining = 10. |  |
| 8 | Who decides whether a patient's dialysis frequency changes near the cap? | The clinical team, not billing | Financial limits must not silently reduce dialysis. |  |
| 9 | Under RA 10173, health data are classified as: | Sensitive personal information | Sensitive information requires stricter protection. |  |
| 10 | A staff member posts a patient's photo in a staff group chat. This is: | A potential privacy breach to report to the DPO | Patient images are personal data. |  |
| 11 | National Privacy Commission (NPC) rules require notification of qualifying breaches within: | 72 hours of knowledge | The DPO determines whether notification applies. |  |
| 12 | Forty claims are denied for signature dates before treatment dates. The root cause is most likely: | Batch pre-signing of forms | Workflow, not paperwork, caused the denials. |  |
| 13 | What should happen to denied claims? | Analysis by reason code, correction and resubmission where allowed | Denial patterns reveal fixable workflow gaps. |  |
| 14 | PhilHealth paid ₱27 billion for HD claims in the first half of 2025. The management implication is: | Increasing claims scrutiny and need for clean documentation | Large spending attracts audit attention. |  |
| 15 | Which items must you verify with the PRO before training staff? | Current claim forms, submission system, filing windows and inpatient rules | Payer details change and need local confirmation. |  |

## Module 11: Budgeting, Cost per Treatment, and Cash

| Q | Stem | Keyed answer | Explanation | Objective |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Which is a variable cost in a dialysis unit? | Bloodlines | Bloodlines are consumed with each session. |  |
| 2 | Fixed ₱920,000, variable ₱2,100, 400 sessions. Cost per treatment? | ₱4,400 | (920,000 + 840,000) ÷ 400 = 4,400. |  |
| 3 | Why does cost per treatment rise when volume falls? | Fixed costs spread over fewer sessions | Fixed costs per session increase as volume drops. |  |
| 4 | Net revenue ₱6,350, variable ₱2,100. Contribution margin per session? | ₱4,250 | 6,350 − 2,100 = 4,250. |  |
| 5 | Fixed ₱920,000 and margin ₱4,250. Break-even sessions per month? | 217 | 920,000 ÷ 4,250 = 216.5, rounded up. |  |
| 6 | What is a step cost? | A cost that jumps at a capacity threshold, like an extra shift team | Step costs appear when volume crosses a staffing or capacity limit. |  |
| 7 | Net revenue ₱80,000 a day and 60 average collection days. Cash tied up in receivables? | ₱4.8 million | 80,000 × 60 = 4,800,000. |  |
| 8 | The income statement shows profit but payroll cannot be met. The most likely cause is: | Slow collections and growing receivables | Profit and cash differ in timing. |  |
| 9 | What does accounts receivable aging show? | Unpaid amounts grouped by how long they have been outstanding | Aging reveals collection delays. |  |
| 10 | Which budget approach is acceptable? | Show trade-offs and keep safety controls, letting governance decide | Safety controls are not negotiable line items. |  |
| 11 | A missed session affects finances because: | Revenue is lost while fixed costs continue | Fixed costs are incurred regardless of attendance. |  |
| 12 | Recognized revenue differs from cash collected because: | Revenue is recognized when earned; cash arrives when paid | Timing and denials create the gap. |  |
| 13 | Before promising 45 more sessions a month from a new shift, you should: | Check whether the new shift's staffing cost exceeds the added contribution | Step costs can erase the margin of added volume. |  |
| 14 | Why use your own ledger rather than published cost studies? | Local prices change quickly and published Philippine data are scarce | Current local data give a defensible base. |  |
| 15 | Which item belongs in a sensitivity analysis? | Margin and cash if volume drops 10% or collections slow by 30 days | Sensitivity shows risk under plausible changes. |  |

## Module 12: Continuity During Outages and Disasters

| Q | Stem | Keyed answer | Explanation | Objective |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Before a forecast typhoon, early dialysis was associated in the Sandy study with: | Lower odds of death within 30 days | About 28% lower adjusted odds of 30-day death. |  |
| 2 | Fuel 300 L, consumption 20 L/h. Generator runtime? | 15 hours | 300 ÷ 20 = 15. |  |
| 3 | Twelve machines at 500 mL/min dialysate flow need how much treated water per hour (before other uses)? | 360 L | 12 × 0.5 L/min × 60 = 360 L/h. |  |
| 4 | Generator runtime 18 hours, water autonomy 5 hours. Operating autonomy? | 5 hours | The smaller value binds. |  |
| 5 | After a flood, before resuming treatment, you must: | Have the water system tested and released | Floods can contaminate water and damage treatment components. |  |
| 6 | A partner-unit agreement should specify: | Referral process, records that travel and billing arrangements | Complete terms make transfers work under pressure. |  |
| 7 | Which record must travel with a transferred patient? | Prescription, serology, access type and Philippine Health Insurance Corporation (PhilHealth) Dialysis Database (PDD) status | The receiving unit needs these to treat safely. |  |
| 8 | Generator testing should be done: | Under load, on schedule | No-load tests miss failures that appear under demand. |  |
| 9 | Why keep a printed patient contact list? | Electronic systems may be unavailable during outages | Redundant communication tools are essential. |  |
| 10 | When should typhoon season preparation begin? | Before the season, with generator, fuel, water and supply checks | Most preparation cannot be done in the last 24 hours. |  |
| 11 | During recovery, which patients are treated first? | Those at highest clinical risk, as decided by clinical leads | Prioritize by physiology. |  |
| 12 | In the brownout case, the binding constraint was: | Treated water | Water ran out before fuel. |  |
| 13 | Asia-Pacific disaster reviews emphasized: | Patient tracking, transfer arrangements, emergency supplies and staff welfare | Lessons repeat across disasters in the region. |  |
| 14 | Who decides whether to provide early dialysis before a typhoon? | The Medical Director and clinical team | It is a clinical decision supported by operations. |  |
| 15 | A partner-unit agreement is best validated by: | Sending a transient patient through the full process annually | A tested agreement works under pressure. |  |

## Module 13: Running a Freestanding Unit

| Q | Stem | Keyed answer | Explanation | Objective |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | A freestanding unit secures emergency transfer through: | Written agreements with nearby hospitals and ambulance providers | Formal agreements make transfer reliable. |  |
| 2 | Which permit is typically needed besides the DOH license? | Fire safety inspection certificate | Local permits are part of the evidence index. |  |
| 3 | Used dialyzers and bloodlines are classified as: | Infectious healthcare waste | Blood-contaminated items require infectious-waste handling. |  |
| 4 | What document records each waste pickup? | A manifest | Manifests trace waste to treatment. |  |
| 5 | General waste placed in infectious-waste bags mainly causes: | Higher disposal costs | Infectious waste disposal is more expensive. |  |
| 6 | A monthly owner report should include: | Safety, quality, operations, finance, compliance and decisions needed | A consistent structure builds trust. |  |
| 7 | In a multisite network, what should be standardized? | Policies, forms, indicator definitions and supplier contracts | Standardize what must be the same; vary only with justification. |  |
| 8 | The transfer case failed mainly because of: | Single points of failure in hospital and ambulance arrangements | Redundancy prevents delays. |  |
| 9 | Laboratory contracts should specify: | Turnaround times for results such as serology | Late results delay isolation and clinical decisions. |  |
