| 10 | Comparing freestanding and hospital units, evidence suggests: | Systems behind the structure matter more than the setting itself | Observational data show variation within both settings. |  |
| 11 | Who leads infection prevention in a freestanding unit? | The Medical Director, through the unit program | Without a hospital committee, the unit program fills that role. |  |
| 12 | How often should a transfer drill be run? | Regularly, for example quarterly | Regular drills keep arrangements working. |  |
| 13 | Rotating managers across network sites must not result in: | A site without a named accountable manager on any day | Accountability needs a named person daily. |  |
| 14 | Where should permit expiry dates be tracked? | In the evidence index with owners and status colors | The evidence index prevents surprise expiries. |  |
| 15 | Why do owners who trust the numbers rarely override safety decisions? | Consistent factual reporting builds confidence in the manager's judgment | Transparency reduces conflict. |  |

| Q | Stem | Keyed answer | Explanation | Objective |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | In I-PASS, what does the final "S" stand for? | Synthesis by receiver | The receiver reads back and confirms understanding. |  |
| 2 | The I-PASS multicenter study found preventable adverse events fell by about: | 30% | Without lengthening handoffs. |  |
| 3 | An emergent request (severe hyperkalemia) arrives while all stations are booked. The rule is: | Treat immediately, displacing the least urgent session with attending agreement | Emergent physiology takes priority. |  |
| 4 | Why reserve an inpatient block of capacity? | To avoid displacing outpatients daily | Planned capacity absorbs predictable inpatient demand. |  |
| 5 | A ward nurse without dialysis competencies is floated to the unit. You should: | Assign non-dialysis support tasks only | Competency limits apply even on short-staffed days. |  |
| 6 | A patient arrives having taken insulin and not eaten. The main risk during dialysis is: | Hypoglycemia | Dialysis removes glucose; insulin without food compounds the risk. |  |
| 7 | Serology status is unknown for an urgent inpatient. The safe approach is: | Treat under precautions at an isolation-capable station until results return | Unknown status is managed as potentially infectious. |  |

| 12 | The return handoff from dialysis to ward should include: | Fluid removed, events and post-dialysis instructions | The ward needs to know what happened. |  |
| 13 | Adapting I-PASS to dialysis handoffs is considered: | Evidence-supported inference from resident handoff research | The principle generalizes, but direct trials are lacking. |  |
| 14 | What is the most common cause of delayed inpatient dialysis? | Transport delays and incomplete preparation | Interfaces, not machines, usually fail. |  |
| 15 | After the unprepared-patient case, the durable fix was: | A request form with cutoff, preparation checklist and structured handoff, audited weekly | System changes prevent recurrence. |  |

| Q | Stem | Keyed answer | Explanation | Objective |
|---|---|---|---|---|

| 2 | Six separations, average headcount 20, over 12 months. Annual turnover? | 30% | 6 ÷ 20 × 100 = 30%. |  |
| 3 | Three of 20 budgeted positions are vacant. Vacancy rate? | 15% | 3 ÷ 20 × 100 = 15%. |  |
| 4 | Turnover over six months was 20%. Annualized turnover is about: | 40% | 20% × (12 ÷ 6) = 40%. |  |
| 5 | What do burnout intervention meta-analyses suggest? | Programs can reduce emotional exhaustion, best paired with workload fixes | Effects were moderate to large, with variable study quality. |  |
| 6 | The first step of a coaching conversation is: | State specifically what you observed | Specific observation keeps the conversation factual. |  |
| 7 | Where should correction usually happen? | In private | Public correction damages trust. |  |
| 8 | A patient's complaint about a prescription goes to: | The Medical Director | Clinical concerns require clinical review. |  |
| 9 | A good grievance process acknowledges complaints within about: | 24 hours | Prompt acknowledgment reduces escalation. |  |
| 10 | In the field case, the patient's anger was driven by: | Lost income from delayed start times caused by shift overruns | Listening revealed a system cause. |  |
| 11 | Annualized turnover of 70% mainly threatens: | Competency stability and safety | Constant replacement keeps the team inexperienced. |  |
| 12 | Who decides to transfer an aggressive patient to another unit? | The Medical Director, as a last resort | Transfer affects access to dialysis and needs clinical judgment. |  |
| 13 | Translated patient experience surveys without validation should be: | Described as unvalidated when results are reported | Honesty about measurement quality protects interpretation. |  |
| 14 | What makes staff trust that complaints matter? | Seeing complaints lead to system fixes | Visible improvement builds engagement. |  |
| 15 | Burnout is best treated by managers as: | A signal of workload and system problems | System causes need system fixes. |  |

| Q | Stem | Keyed answer | Explanation | Objective |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | What does the three-way match compare? | Purchase order, receiving report and invoice | Payment follows agreement among the three documents. |  |
| 2 | Total receivables ₱4.5 million; average daily revenue ₱90,000. DSO? | 50 days | 4,500,000 ÷ 90,000 = 50. |  |
| 3 | A rising over-90-day receivables bucket usually indicates: | Unsubmitted, returned or unresolved claims | Old receivables need claim-by-claim work. |  |
| 4 | In an imprest petty cash system, replenishment is: | Based on receipts, restoring the fund to its fixed amount | Receipts plus cash must always equal the fund. |  |
| 5 | In a small team where one person both records and reconciles, a compensating control is: | Independent monthly review by the owner or an outside accountant | Independent review offsets the overlap. |  |
| 6 | What is unapplied cash? | Payments received but not yet matched to specific claims | It must be resolved, not left indefinitely. |  |
| 7 | Which step comes first in the month-end close? | Lock the session count for the month | Everything reconciles back to delivered sessions. |  |
| 8 | Fraud surveys most commonly cite which weakness? | Lack of internal controls or override of existing controls | Controls that are actually performed matter most. |  |
| 9 | ESA vials are 40 short at month-end. The first step is to: | Compare administration records with issue records to locate the gap | Evidence before conclusions. |  |
| 10 | Who should not be the same person in a strong control system? | The person who pays and the person who reconciles the bank account | Separation prevents concealment. |  |
| 11 | Under RA 11976 (2024), the primary document for sales of services became: | The invoice | Confirm current requirements with your accountant. |  |
| 12 | Revenue for 90 days is ₱8.1 million. Average daily revenue? | ₱90,000 | 8,100,000 ÷ 90 = 90,000. |  |
| 13 | High-value inventory such as ESAs should be counted: | At least monthly, more often after a variance | Frequent counts detect problems early. |  |
| 14 | A variance report explains: | Material differences between actual and budget | Explanations drive corrective action. |  |
| 15 | Following a session forward through the ledger allows you to: | Prove every delivered session became a claim, cash or a documented denial | Reconciliation closes the loop from care to cash. |  |

| 3 | Night differential applies to hours worked: | Between 10 PM and 6 AM | Art. 86 defines the night period. |  |
| 4 | Hourly ₱100; four night hours on an ordinary day. Night differential? | ₱40 | 4 × 100 × 0.10 = 40. |  |
| 5 | Pay for 8 hours worked on a regular holiday at a daily rate of ₱800 is: | ₱1,600 | 200% of the daily rate. |  |

| 9 | A missing time punch should be corrected: | With evidence, employee acknowledgment and reviewer sign-off as a new entry | Corrections must be auditable. |  |

| 12 | A ghost employee is detected by reconciling: | Register headcount to the active employee list | Every paid person must be an active employee. |  |

| 14 | Government dialysis staff premiums are determined by: | CSC, DBM and agency rules under their appointment | Public-sector rules differ. |  |
| 15 | Gross pay minus authorized employee deductions equals: | Net pay | Employer shares are tracked separately. |  |

## Module 19: Integrated Readiness and Improvement Capstone

| Q | Stem | Keyed answer | Explanation | Objective |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | A typhoon is forecast in 72 hours. Who decides on early dialysis? | The Medical Director and clinical team | Early dialysis is a clinical decision supported by operations. |  |
| 2 | A loop culture is 70 CFU/mL with action level 50 and maximum 100. The correct response is: | Action-level response: disinfect, retest and inform the Medical Director | Action levels exist to prevent reaching the maximum. |  |
| 3 | The owner proposes closing two days before the storm. The best reply is: | Propose schedule compression with staffing and cost implications | Closure creates dangerous gaps; compression protects patients. |  |
| 4 | Which calculator tells you whether water or fuel limits operations during an outage? | CALC-12 | Outage autonomy compares generator runtime and water storage. |  |
| 5 | A patient has used 152 of 156 sessions in late November. The right action is to: | Use CALC-07, counsel the patient early and inform the attending | Financial limits must not silently change treatment. |  |
| 6 | Which is a safety-critical failure that stops the capstone? | Bypassing water release | Clearance bypass is a stop item. |  |
| 7 | Two nurses resign effective Friday. The first quantitative step is: | Run CALC-01 for the revised schedule | Numbers define the gap and the options. |  |
| 8 | Returned claims during a crisis week should be: | Assigned with a correction deadline after immediate safety work | Prioritize safety, but do not lose the revenue. |  |
| 9 | Holiday and overtime pay for extra shifts should be computed with: | CALC-10 | Premium pay rules depend on day type and hours. |  |
| 10 | What makes a decision log useful afterward? | Times, decisions, owners and outcomes recorded as they happen | Contemporaneous records support review and accountability. |  |
| 11 | Which patients are on the high-risk list before a storm? | Long-gap, high potassium, catheter-dependent and new-start patients | Prioritize by physiologic risk. |  |
| 12 | Portfolio artifacts must use: | Fictional or de-identified data | Privacy rules apply to training materials. |  |
| 13 | A float nurse without signed dialysis competencies is offered. You should: | Use her only for tasks within her competencies | Competency limits hold in emergencies too. |  |
| 14 | After the storm, treatment resumes only when: | Power, water quality and staffing are confirmed and released | Phased reopening follows release. |  |
| 15 | The central skill the capstone tests is: | Prioritizing by patient risk and routing each decision to its authorized owner with evidence | Integration, routing and evidence define effective management. |  |

# Attending Nephrologist

## Module 1: Taking attending responsibility

| Q | Stem | Keyed answer | Explanation | Objective |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | What should be settled before the first shift? | Written primary, backup and transfer arrangements | An order set cannot allocate responsibility when a clinician is unavailable or a patient needs transfer. |  |
| 2 | Which document defines whether the facility may operate an HD service? | The facility’s applicable DOH License to Operate | A facility license, physician credentials and payer accreditation establish different authorities. |  |
| 3 | Under the PSN 2024 HD guidelines, how often should the AN review a maintenance HD patient? | At least monthly, preferably face to face, with telemedicine when necessary | Section 1.2 sets a minimum monthly consultation; documentation of each review supports continuity and claims integrity. |  |
| 4 | A clinic’s night POD cannot reach you during an emergency. What design element was missing? | A time-limited escalation rule to a named backup and an emergency transfer pathway | Escalation must not depend on one person answering; a time trigger converts an unanswered call into an action. |  |
| 5 | One physician serves as both the AN and the unit’s Medical Director. What should the record show? | Which role is acting for each decision | Individual patient care and facility oversight carry different duties; naming the acting role keeps accountability clear. |  |

## Module 2: The renal consultation and diagnostic dataset

| Q | Stem | Keyed answer | Explanation | Objective |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Which finding most strengthens a glomerular explanation? | Dysmorphic erythrocytes and red-cell casts | Sediment morphology localizes injury to the glomerular capillary wall in a way creatinine cannot. |  |
| 2 | Which data clarify whether a creatinine change is acute? | A dated baseline and a serial trajectory | Change over time classifies acuity and gauges progression. |  |
| 3 | Why does a single creatinine underestimate injury early in AKI? | Creatinine takes 24–72 hours to reach a new steady state after GFR falls | Production continues while filtration falls; the serum level rises gradually toward the new equilibrium. |  |
| 4 | In which patient is cystatin C most useful for estimating GFR? | A bilateral above-knee amputee with low muscle mass | Cystatin C is less dependent on muscle mass, so it corrects the overestimation of GFR from creatinine in sarcopenia or amputation. |  |
| 5 | Muddy-brown granular casts most strongly suggest: | Acute tubular injury | Sloughed tubular epithelial cells form granular casts; prerenal states typically show a bland sediment. |  |

## Module 3: Consultant communication and coverage

| Q | Stem | Keyed answer | Explanation | Objective |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Which detail closes the communication loop? | Read-back by the person assigned to act | Acknowledgement and read-back establish who will act and on what instruction. |  |
| 2 | What should a POD communicate during an HD emergency? | Vital trends, timing, circuit and access findings, interventions and response | The consultant needs trend and action data to advise disposition. |  |
| 3 | A remote consultant says ’monitor closely.’ What is missing? | A specific action, owner, reassessment time and fallback | Vague instructions transfer worry, not responsibility. |  |
| 4 | What does the Data Privacy Act’s proportionality principle require during transfer? | Sending only the clinical data the receiver needs, via an approved channel | Minimum necessary disclosure through approved channels balances continuity with privacy. |  |
| 5 | A patient with presyncope insists on leaving. What must happen before discharge against advice? | A documented assessment of decision-making capacity for that decision | Capacity is decision-specific; if present, an informed refusal is documented with a safety plan. |  |

## Module 4: Consent, prescribing and medication reconciliation

| Q | Stem | Keyed answer | Explanation | Objective |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Which medication instruction supports safe follow-up? | A defined review date, trigger and owner | Explicit monitoring and responsibility reduce both unsafe exposure and unintended long-term omission. |  |
| 2 | How should the physician check consent comprehension? | Ask the patient to explain the options and agreed plan in their own words | Teach-back reveals misunderstandings while there is time to address them. |  |
| 3 | Two weeks after starting an SGLT2 inhibitor, creatinine rises 18% with stable potassium. Best action? | Continue and recheck; this is the expected hemodynamic effect | Reduced intraglomerular pressure produces an early, reversible eGFR dip associated with long-term kidney protection. |  |
| 4 | Which drug property most predicts substantial removal by high-flux hemodialysis (HD)? | Low protein binding with a small volume of distribution | Unbound drug in plasma water crosses the membrane; drug sequestered in tissue is not reached. |  |
| 5 | Under KDIGO 2024, metformin should be stopped when eGFR falls below: | 30 mL/min/1.73 m² | The dose is reduced below 45 and stopped below 30 because of lactic acidosis risk. |  |

## Module 5: Acute kidney injury and post-AKI care

| Q | Stem | Keyed answer | Explanation | Objective |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | What should determine the next step after initial stabilization? | Serial perfusion, urine output, chemistry and complications | Evolving physiology and complications determine urgency while etiologic work-up proceeds. |  |
| 2 | What belongs in the post-AKI discharge plan? | Residual dysfunction, medication changes and named lab follow-up | The next clinician needs a defined action and time frame. |  |
| 3 | Which electrolyte pattern is characteristic of early leptospirosis-associated AKI? | Non-oliguric AKI with hypokalemia from tubular potassium wasting | Leptospiral injury to the proximal tubule and thick ascending limb produces sodium and potassium wasting. |  |
| 4 | What did BICARICU-2 show in severe metabolic acidemia with stage 2–3 AKI? | No reduction in 90-day mortality, with later and less frequent KRT in the bicarbonate group | The primary outcome was neutral; the KRT finding is secondary and hypothesis-generating. |  |
| 5 | Why does venous congestion worsen AKI? | It lowers the transrenal perfusion gradient and raises pressure inside the encapsulated kidney | Perfusion depends on arterial minus venous pressure; a tense, encapsulated kidney compounds the drop. |  |

## Module 6: Potassium, sodium and acid-base emergencies

| Q | Stem | Keyed answer | Explanation | Objective |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Which disposition is appropriate for potassium 7.1 mmol/L with a broad QRS in an HD patient? | Emergency monitored management and same-day dialysis | ECG toxicity signals immediate arrhythmia risk and requires urgent stabilization and definitive removal. |  |
| 2 | What must be anticipated when correcting chronic severe hyponatremia? | Overcorrection risk and a serial monitoring plan with a rescue strategy | Duration, risk factors and an evolving water diuresis determine safety. |  |
| 3 | Why is a 10% glucose infusion added after insulin-glucose when baseline glucose is below 7 mmol/L? | To prevent delayed hypoglycemia, which can occur hours after insulin | Insulin’s hypoglycemic effect outlasts the initial glucose bolus, especially in chronic kidney disease (CKD) with reduced insulin clearance. |  |
| 4 | Which treatment actually removes potassium from the body? | Hemodialysis | Calcium protects the membrane, insulin and salbutamol shift potassium; only gut, kidney or dialysis remove it. |  |
| 5 | HCO₃⁻ 12 mmol/L with pCO₂ 38 mmHg indicates: | Metabolic acidosis with a concomitant respiratory acidosis | Winter’s formula predicts pCO₂ of 26 ± 2; a value of 38 means ventilation is inadequate. |  |

## Module 7: Acute kidney replacement and ICU consultation

| Q | Stem | Keyed answer | Explanation | Objective |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | What most strengthens the acute KRT indication? | Worsening refractory organ-threatening fluid overload | Indications depend on the clinical complication and its response to treatment. |  |
| 2 | What belongs in the acute KRT order review? | An indication, a feasible modality, reassessment and stopping criteria | The prescription must address the indication and evolve with illness and recovery. |  |
| 3 | What did STARRT-AKI show? | No 90-day mortality benefit from accelerated initiation, with more dialysis dependence among survivors | Accelerated starts exposed patients who would have recovered to KRT without survival benefit. |  |
| 4 | Which patient most clearly favors CRRT over intermittent HD? | Traumatic brain injury with raised intracranial pressure | Gradual solute removal avoids rapid osmotic shifts that can worsen cerebral edema. |  |
| 5 | Which sign during KRT most suggests renal recovery? | Rising spontaneous urine output with falling interdialytic creatinine | Native filtration returning shows up as urine and as creatinine that no longer climbs between sessions. |  |

## Module 8: CRRT delivery, special indications and stopping

| Q | Stem | Keyed answer | Explanation | Objective |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Which measure best reveals a delivery gap? | Actual run time and delivered effluent dose | Interruptions can materially reduce delivered treatment despite a correct order. |  |
| 2 | Which team should review drug exposure after an unexpected CRRT interruption? | The treating team with pharmacy input | Delivered therapy affects drug clearance and can change exposure. |  |
| 3 | A 70-kg patient receives 2,100 mL/h effluent for 12 of 24 hours. What is the delivered dose? | About 15 mL/kg/h | 2,100 x 12 / 24 / 70 = 15 mL/kg/h, below the 20–25 mL/kg/h target. |  |
| 4 | During regional citrate anticoagulation, a total-to-ionized calcium ratio of 2.8 with worsening acidosis suggests: | Citrate accumulation from impaired metabolism | Unmetabolized citrate binds ionized calcium while total calcium rises, widening the ratio. |  |
| 5 | What did the RENAL and ATN trials show about CRRT intensity? | Higher intensity did not improve survival compared with standard intensity | Both trials support a delivered dose of 20–25 mL/kg/h without escalation. |  |

## Module 9: CKD staging, progression and risk reduction

| Q | Stem | Keyed answer | Explanation | Objective |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Which measure adds important prognostic information to eGFR? | Urine albumin-to-creatinine ratio | Albuminuria contributes independent prognostic information and can influence treatment. |  |
| 2 | Which finding helps establish CKD chronicity? | Persistent abnormalities documented over more than 3 months | Serial records separate chronic disease from acute changes. |  |
| 3 | How do SGLT2 inhibitors lower intraglomerular pressure? | By restoring macula densa sodium delivery, activating tubuloglomerular feedback and constricting the afferent arteriole | Feedback-mediated afferent constriction complements efferent dilation from RAS blockade. |  |
| 4 | What did CONFIDENCE demonstrate? | Greater UACR reduction with combined finerenone and empagliflozin than either alone (a surrogate outcome) | The trial was powered for albuminuria change; hard outcomes remain to be shown. |  |
| 5 | A KFRE 2-year risk above 40% should prompt: | Preparation for kidney replacement therapy, including access and transplant planning | KDIGO 2024 uses a 2-year risk above 40% to trigger KRT preparation. |  |

## Module 10: CKD complications and multidisciplinary review

| Q | Stem | Keyed answer | Explanation | Objective |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Why review transferrin saturation with ferritin? | Inflammation can raise ferritin despite restricted iron availability | The two measures describe different aspects of iron availability and must be read alongside illness. |  |
| 2 | How should CKD-MBD labs guide reassessment? | Interpret calcium, phosphate and PTH trends together | Trends and related measures support a coordinated plan. |  |
| 3 | Per KDIGO 2026, what is first-line therapy for renal anemia once correctable causes are addressed? | An ESA, with HIF-PHIs as alternatives in selected situations | Long-term cardiovascular and thrombotic uncertainty keeps HIF-PHIs second line. |  |
| 4 | In which patient should a HIF-PHI be avoided? | A patient with active malignancy or a recent thrombotic event | HIF stabilization may promote angiogenesis and thrombosis; KDIGO advises against use in these settings. |  |
| 5 | Why did trials targeting normal hemoglobin with ESA change practice? | They showed more cardiovascular events or stroke without clear benefit | CHOIR and TREAT reported harm with higher targets, so treatment aims to avoid symptoms and transfusion rather than normalize hemoglobin. |  |

## Module 11: Glomerular disease and kidney biopsy decisions

| Q | Stem | Keyed answer | Explanation | Objective |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Which action fits suspected pulmonary-renal syndrome? | Urgent multidisciplinary assessment and transfer coordination | A pulmonary-renal presentation can involve life-threatening organ injury and needs urgent evaluation. |  |
| 2 | What makes kidney biopsy most useful? | A reasonable chance of changing management at an acceptable procedural risk | Clinical impact and safety guide biopsy selection. |  |
| 3 | What did PEXIVAS show? | Plasma exchange did not reduce death or kidney failure overall; reduced-dose steroids were noninferior with fewer infections | These findings reshaped KDIGO 2024 toward selective plasma exchange and lower steroid exposure. |  |
| 4 | Which screening is essential before rituximab? | Hepatitis B surface antigen and core antibody | Rituximab can reactivate occult hepatitis B, sometimes fatally; core-antibody-positive patients need monitoring or prophylaxis. |  |
| 5 | Why are nephrotic patients prone to thrombosis? | Urinary loss of antithrombin with increased hepatic clotting-factor synthesis | The coagulation balance tips toward clotting as anticoagulant proteins leak and procoagulants rise. |  |

## Module 12: Tubulointerstitial, vascular and inherited disease

| Q | Stem | Keyed answer | Explanation | Objective |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Who evaluates suitability of a potentially affected sibling as donor? | The transplant donor team through an independent evaluation | Independent assessment addresses donor safety, inherited risk and voluntariness. |  |
| 2 | What should prompt urgent assessment for thrombotic microangiopathy? | AKI with thrombocytopenia and evidence of hemolysis | The combination raises concern for organ-threatening vascular injury. |  |
| 3 | Why is ADAMTS13 sent before giving plasma in suspected TMA? | Plasma replaces ADAMTS13 and can mask the diagnosis of TTP | A pre-treatment level distinguishes TTP from complement-mediated and other TMAs. |  |
| 4 | What is the mechanism of tolvaptan in ADPKD? | Vasopressin V2-receptor blockade reduces cyclic AMP-driven cyst growth | Lower cyclic AMP slows cell proliferation and fluid secretion into cysts. |  |
| 5 | Which feature suggests drug-induced interstitial nephritis? | Sterile pyuria and white-cell casts after starting a proton pump inhibitor | Interstitial inflammation produces a leukocyte-predominant sediment with low-grade proteinuria. |  |

## Module 13: Kidney failure planning and dialysis initiation

| Q | Stem | Keyed answer | Explanation | Objective |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | What justifies starting dialysis in an otherwise uncertain case? | A patient-specific indication supported by trajectory and complications | A start decision integrates clinical indications, feasibility and patient goals. |  |
| 2 | Which action supports planned kidney failure care? | Early access planning and transplant referral aligned with goals | Preparation gives patients time to choose and services time to coordinate. |  |
| 3 | What did the IDEAL trial show? | No survival benefit from starting dialysis at a higher eGFR | Symptom-guided starts are as safe as early, number-based starts. |  |
| 4 | Why protect residual kidney function after dialysis starts? | It provides continuous middle-molecule and phosphate clearance and is associated with better survival | Native clearance works 24 hours a day and eases fluid and diet limits. |  |
| 5 | Which timeline is most appropriate for creating an arteriovenous fistula? | Months before the expected start, because maturation takes time | Maturation commonly takes weeks to months, and some fistulas need intervention before use. |  |

## Module 14: Maintenance HD prescription and delivered adequacy

| Q | Stem | Keyed answer | Explanation | Objective |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | What is a sound first step in falling adequacy with frequent alarms? | Check delivered time, sampling and access function | Confirm the reason for inadequate delivery before changing the prescription. |  |
| 2 | What should a target-weight revision consider? | Symptoms, examination, blood pressure trend and treatment tolerance | Volume goals balance congestion against harms of excessive fluid removal. |  |
| 3 | What is the KDOQI 2015 minimum delivered spKt/V for thrice-weekly HD? | 1.2 | The target is 1.4 to keep delivered dose above the 1.2 minimum. |  |
| 4 | Why is equilibrated Kt/V lower than single-pool Kt/V? | Urea rebounds from tissues into blood after dialysis ends | Delayed transfer from intracellular compartments raises post-dialysis urea. |  |
| 5 | Which statement about hemodiafiltration evidence is accurate? | CONVINCE showed lower mortality with high-dose HDF; confirmation from H4RT is pending | A single large trial supports benefit at convection volumes of at least 23 L. |  |

## Module 15: HD emergencies and consultant escalation

| Q | Stem | Keyed answer | Explanation | Objective |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | What is the priority when several patients deteriorate concurrently? | Activate emergency response and alert leadership to a possible shared hazard | Clustering changes the immediate risk to other patients and requires parallel unit action. |  |
| 2 | Why is circuit blood not returned in suspected hemolysis? | It carries free hemoglobin and released potassium that can cause arrhythmia | Lysed red cells release potassium; reinfusion adds to the cardiac risk. |  |
| 3 | Immediate positioning for suspected venous air embolism: | Left side down with head down, after clamping the venous line | This position aims to trap air in the right atrium and away from the pulmonary outflow. |  |
| 4 | First steps for intradialytic hypotension: | Stop ultrafiltration, lay flat and give a small saline bolus within protocol | Removing the cause and restoring blood volume usually resolves it; recurrent episodes need target-weight review. |  |
| 5 | Which exposure classically causes cluster hemolysis in HD units? | Chloramine breakthrough in the water treatment system | Chloramine oxidizes hemoglobin; carbon tank exhaustion is a classic outbreak cause. |  |

## Module 16: HD access and infection prevention

| Q | Stem | Keyed answer | Explanation | Objective |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Which action matters most in an unstable patient with suspected access sepsis? | Emergency stabilization and coordinated infection management | Instability requires immediate intervention and source assessment. |  |
| 2 | What makes a vascular access life-plan individualized? | It accounts for treatment trajectory, anatomy and patient goals | Selection should anticipate current and future access needs. |  |
| 3 | What did LOCK IT-100 show? | A 71% lower risk of catheter-related bloodstream infection with taurolidine-heparin lock | The primary endpoint was time to adjudicated catheter-related bloodstream infection. |  |
| 4 | Which organism usually requires catheter removal in catheter-related bloodstream infection? | Staphylococcus aureus | S. aureus, Pseudomonas and Candida infections are poorly cleared from biofilm. |  |
| 5 | Why compare infection rates per 1,000 catheter-days rather than raw counts? | The denominator adjusts for how many patients had catheters and for how long | Exposure-based rates allow fair comparison over time. |  |

## Module 17: Peritoneal dialysis prescription and home follow-up

| Q | Stem | Keyed answer | Explanation | Objective |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Which information is essential in a PD review? | Symptoms, fluid balance, residual function and delivered exchanges | Goal-directed assessment integrates delivery, residual clearance and patient outcomes. |  |
| 2 | How should the team respond to recurring missed exchanges? | Identify practical barriers and redesign support with the patient | Addressing schedule, training and supply barriers improves deliverability. |  |
| 3 | A fast transporter on a long glucose dwell is most likely to experience: | Fluid reabsorption and poor ultrafiltration | The glucose gradient dissipates quickly, so fluid is reabsorbed late in the dwell; icodextrin helps. |  |
| 4 | What did ADEMEX show about peritoneal small-solute clearance? | Raising peritoneal clearance above moderate levels did not improve survival | This supports goal-directed rather than number-driven prescribing. |  |
| 5 | Why does early hypertonic dwell cause sodium sieving? | Aquaporins move free water without sodium early in the dwell | Water crosses through aquaporins faster than sodium diffuses, lowering dialysate sodium transiently. |  |

## Module 18: PD peritonitis and mechanical complications

| Q | Stem | Keyed answer | Explanation | Objective |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Which workflow is most appropriate for cloudy effluent with pain and fever? | Same-day evaluation, effluent studies and empiric treatment plus follow-up | Early treatment and reassessment reduce delay and help identify refractory or complicated infection. |  |
| 2 | What must happen after starting treatment for suspected PD peritonitis? | Assign culture review and a timely clinical reassessment | Follow-up identifies response, the need to narrow treatment and complications. |  |
| 3 | Which effluent finding meets the ISPD 2022 laboratory criterion? | WBC 350/µL with 80% neutrophils after a 4-hour dwell | The criterion is >100/µL with >50% neutrophils after at least 2 hours. |  |
| 4 | What is the ISPD 2022 target for overall peritonitis rate? | ≤0.40 episodes per patient-year at risk | Units should monitor and report this rate at least annually. |  |
| 5 | When is catheter removal recommended? | Refractory peritonitis not clearing after 5 days of appropriate antibiotics, or fungal peritonitis | Removal protects the membrane and the patient’s life when infection cannot be cleared. |  |

## Module 19: Kidney transplantation referral and shared care

| Q | Stem | Keyed answer | Explanation | Objective |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | What should guide immunosuppression changes in a febrile recipient with rising creatinine? | Urgent transplant-team coordination with evaluation of competing causes | Several high-risk causes can coexist, and uncoordinated changes may worsen outcomes. |  |
| 2 | Which information improves a transplant referral? | Cause and trajectory, comorbidity, sensitizing events and barriers the team can address | The receiving service can prioritize and coordinate a complete evaluation. |  |
| 3 | Clarithromycin raises tacrolimus levels by: | Inhibiting CYP3A4 metabolism | CYP3A4 inhibition reduces clearance, raising levels and nephrotoxicity risk. |  |
| 4 | When does KDIGO 2020 suggest referral for transplant evaluation? | At least 6–12 months before anticipated dialysis, generally eGFR <30 and declining | Early referral enables pre-emptive transplantation. |  |
| 5 | Why is a living donor evaluated by an independent team? | To protect donor safety and ensure the decision is voluntary | Separating donor advocacy from recipient care avoids conflicts of interest. |  |

## Module 20: Conservative kidney management and serious illness

| Q | Stem | Keyed answer | Explanation | Objective |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Which element makes a dialysis trial time limited? | Agreed outcomes and a review date | Explicit measures and timing support informed reassessment of benefit and burden. |  |
| 2 | What is part of comprehensive conservative care? | Active symptom treatment and planned follow-up | Conservative management remains an active clinical pathway. |  |
| 3 | The family asks the physician to decide privately before speaking to the patient. Best response? | Assess the patient’s capacity and involve him in the decision | A capacitated patient holds the decision; family support is welcome but not a substitute. |  |
| 4 | What does observational evidence suggest for very elderly patients with high comorbidity? | Dialysis may confer little survival advantage over conservative care, with more hospital time | These are non-randomized data with selection bias; they inform, not decide. |  |
| 5 | Capacity assessment is best described as: | Decision-specific: understanding, retaining, weighing and communicating a choice | Capacity is assessed for the particular decision at the particular time. |  |

## Module 21: PhilHealth documentation and ethical claims

| Q | Stem | Keyed answer | Explanation | Objective |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | What should the AN do first when records conflict? | Hold attestation and reconcile source records | Truthful attestation depends on a traceable reconciliation of actual services. |  |
| 2 | Which records support verification of an actual dialysis session? | The treatment record and corresponding claim documentation | Claims attest to services delivered on verifiable dates. |  |
| 3 | How many HD sessions per year does PhilHealth cover under Circular 2023-0009? | Up to 156 | The benefit equals three sessions a week for 52 weeks. |  |
| 4 | What is the correct way to fix a duplicated claim entry? | Correct it through the approved workflow with an audit trail | Traceable corrections preserve integrity and protect staff. |  |
| 5 | Which principle governs sharing claims data? | Minimum necessary disclosure through approved channels under the Data Privacy Act | Proportional, secure processing protects patients and the facility. |  |

## Module 22: Disasters, outbreaks and service continuity

| Q | Stem | Keyed answer | Explanation | Objective |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | What determines disposition during an outage? | Clinical urgency and confirmed receiving capability | Safe continuity depends on risk-based triage and verified handoff. |  |
| 2 | When can the unit close an outage tracker entry? | When treatment has resumed or disposition and acceptance are confirmed | Confirmed disposition closes the continuity gap. |  |
| 3 | Which patient is at highest risk after missed sessions? | An anuric patient with prior hyperkalemia who missed two sessions | No urine output and potassium history predict rapid, dangerous accumulation. |  |
| 4 | What is the most useful pre-storm intervention? | Scheduling extra or early dialysis before landfall | A buffer session lowers potassium and fluid before access to care is cut. |  |
| 5 | Why include serologies on the emergency card? | Receiving units need hepatitis B status to place patients safely | Hepatitis B segregation is an infection-prevention requirement in every unit. |  |

## Module 23: Supervision, evidence appraisal and quality improvement

| Q | Stem | Keyed answer | Explanation | Objective |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Which comparison supports assessment of the apparent improvement? | Rates based on comparable catheter exposure | The exposure denominator accounts for a changing population at risk. |  |
| 2 | What is an example of a balancing measure in a catheter-reduction project? | Missed or delayed treatments associated with the change | Balancing measures detect new harms introduced by an improvement. |  |
| 3 | A trial shows event rates of 20% vs 15%. What is the NNT? | 20 | ARR = 5 percentage points; NNT = 1 / 0.05 = 20. |  |
| 4 | Why does the same relative risk reduction yield more benefit in high-risk patients? | Absolute risk reduction scales with baseline risk | A constant relative effect applied to a larger baseline risk removes more events. |  |
| 5 | Which feedback is most useful to a learner? | ’Next time, read back the potassium order before ending the call’ | Specific, behavior-focused feedback gives a repeatable action. |  |

## Module 24: Integrated attending capstone

| Q | Stem | Keyed answer | Explanation | Objective |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Which follow-through is required after the acute transfer? | Confirm receipt, reconcile the erroneous claim and reassign longitudinal follow-up | Continuity includes acceptance, documentation integrity and a named next clinician. |  |
| 2 | What outcome would make the capstone QI claim credible? | An explicitly defined measure compared with baseline at a planned review | Baseline, denominator and review make improvement observable. |  |
| 3 | After stabilization from catheter sepsis, what should happen to his home-therapy preference? | Reopen PD planning and refer for transplant evaluation | An urgent start does not cancel informed modality choice. |  |
| 4 | Which is a critical action in the capstone claims task? | Stopping release of a knowingly inaccurate claim | Integrity takes precedence over deadlines. |  |
| 5 | A missed critical action at a station results in: | Feedback and repeat assessment with a different case | Critical actions are non-compensable. |  |

# Medical Records Officer and Custodian

## Module 1: Role, Designation, and Sources of Authority

| Q | Stem | Keyed answer | Explanation | Objective |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Who may authenticate a nephrologist’s diagnosis? | Treating physician through the clinical workflow | Clinical content belongs to its author. |  |
| 2 | Which body sets the HD package terms? | PhilHealth | Payer terms come from PhilHealth circulars. |  |
| 3 | Which body licenses a hemodialysis facility? | DOH | DOH issues the LTO. |  |
| 4 | PSN’s HD guideline is best described as: | Professional guidance | Strong guidance, not statute. |  |
| 5 | The posted DOH tool’s training certificate requirement applies to facilities with: | More than 20 stations | See the module. |  |
| 6 | A 12-station unit proposes having no designated MRO. Correct response? | Still needs a designated MRO | The threshold concerns the certificate, not the designation. |  |
| 7 | Before acting on an instruction, you check its: | Issuer, scope, and effective date | See the module. |  |
| 8 | A local SOP conflicts with a newer circular. You: | Follow the newer issuance and flag the SOP for revision | See the module. |  |
| 9 | Who decides whether a breach is notified to the NPC? | Personal information controller, advised by the DPO | See the module. |  |
| 10 | Who approves destruction of records? | Facility head with counsel (and NAP for public entities) | See the module. |  |
| 11 | Two HD sessions, September 30 and October 9, 2024, fall under: | Different rates | The rate changed October 9, 2024. |  |
| 12 | The MRO’s role in a coding dispute is to: | Log, preserve, and route to the coder and physician | See the module. |  |
| 13 | Which document is NOT part of the MRO role file? | Patient’s lab results | See the module. |  |
| 14 | A named deputy is needed because: | Custody must continue during absence | See the module. |  |
| 15 | The Data Privacy Act treats health information as: | Sensitive personal information | See the module. |  |

| 14 | "Authorized → delivered → safe" maps to: | Order → run sheet → technical logs | See the module. |  |

## Module 4: Capture, Indexing, Authentication, and Corrections

| Q | Stem | Keyed answer | Explanation | Objective |
